## P001 — Explicit decision owner, consumer, deadline, and action authority

**Ceremony vs property.** A steering committee, sign-off sheet, or RACI is optional; an accountable consequence-bearing authority is not.

**Contrary evidence.** S099

**Critical sources.** S098; S099; S101; S102

**Status at this source freeze.** DECISION_FRAME_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Only information capable of discriminating alternatives is required on the cheap path; provenance, timing, and material omissions must be known when formal analysis is used.

**Decision or consumer.** The accountable decision owner and implementers who must act on the result.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** A model, dashboard, or recommendation has no live decision consumer, no deadline, or ambiguous authority, so analysis cannot control action legitimately.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** A named person or body must own the decision, be able to accept consequences, and have a deadline or review point. Analysts and solvers are not authority by default.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S100; S101; S102

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** LOW

**Contrary evidence strength.** MODERATE

**Empirical comparative strength.** MODERATE

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** MODERATE

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** LOW

**Synthesis note.** Strong practice and implementation support; authority structures remain domain-specific.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Fewer category errors, fewer dominated or infeasible commitments, and a traceable basis for choosing, deferring, escalating, or preserving options.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** A model, dashboard, or recommendation has no live decision consumer, no deadline, or ambiguous authority, so analysis cannot control action legitimately.

**Historical origin.** Wartime operational research's mission-facing teams; post-war decision analysis and systems analysis; current NASA/DoD trade-study guidance.

**How the property evolved.** Command/client attachment evolved into explicit decision ownership, governance, human-in-the-loop authority, and model-maintenance responsibility.

**Important criticisms.** Decision analysis can be normatively coherent while elicited preferences are unstable or stakeholders disagree; explicitness does not make values objective.

**Known failure modes.** Omitted alternatives, inherited framing, false precision, authority gaps, post-hoc criteria changes, and a model with no live decision consumer.

**Lineage class.** HYBRID

**Evolved form in this source.** Analysis begins with an owner-action-deadline tuple and ends in an implementable choice, deferment, experiment, policy, or documented non-decision.

**Mechanism.** Record the named owner, implementers, deadline/review point, escalation and override authority, and the action the evidence can change.

**Model assumptions.** The represented alternatives and consequences span the live decision sufficiently for the claimed conclusion; framing and elicitation are not neutral facts.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** For low-consequence reversible choices, name one accountable chooser and a decision time; no formal charter is required.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The valued consequence, decision criteria, hard constraints, soft preferences, and non-compensatory limits must be distinguishable and challengeable.

**Open questions.** How should authority be represented when consequences and control are distributed across organisations?

**Original form.** OR analysts were attached to an operation and decision command; decision analysis later made the decision maker and action explicit.

**Primary sources.** S005; S006; S040; S073; S075; S076

**Problem it addressed.** Technically elaborate analysis can be produced without anyone authorised to choose, implement, or bear consequences.

**Property id.** P001

**Property name.** Explicit decision owner, consumer, deadline, and action authority

**Relationship class.** CONVERGENT_PROPERTY

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** The resources needed to analyse and implement each alternative are included where they can change feasibility or ranking.

**Trigger or context.** Any consequential allocation, schedule, optimisation, trade study, forecast-controlled action, or automated policy.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Material ambiguity about consequences, preferences, feasibility, timing, or future information is stated rather than silently converted to precise inputs.

**Requalification.** **Claim ids.** C001

**Fresh proposition.** An operational decision analysis needs an identifiable consumer, feasible action authority and a decision or review time if it is to warrant an implementable choice.

**Fresh note.** Preserve the native operational framing while keeping pure theorem certificates outside the owner requirement.

**Scope.** An application claim is being made; mathematical model existence alone does not require a real owner.

**Evaluation.** Trace the proposed action to a consumer able to choose, implement or explicitly defer it before its useful deadline.

**Witness.** A named dispatcher choosing a feasible schedule supplies a consumer; an optimal schedule nobody can enact does not establish operational benefit.

## P002 — Frame the real decision and consequence before selecting a model

**Ceremony vs property.** A named framing workshop is optional; preserving the distinction between real decision and represented problem is mandatory.

**Contrary evidence.** S043; S045; S046

**Critical sources.** S098; S099; S089; S110

**Status at this source freeze.** DECISION_FRAME_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Only information capable of discriminating alternatives is required on the cheap path; provenance, timing, and material omissions must be known when formal analysis is used.

**Decision or consumer.** The accountable decision owner and implementers who must act on the result.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** The model answers the wrong question, excludes important consequences, or treats a means/proxy as the end.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** A named person or body must own the decision, be able to accept consequences, and have a deadline or review point. Analysts and solvers are not authority by default.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S099; S101

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** MODERATE

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** MODERATE

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** MODERATE

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** LOW

**Synthesis note.** Strongly retained, while descriptive research shows framing itself is contestable.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Fewer category errors, fewer dominated or infeasible commitments, and a traceable basis for choosing, deferring, escalating, or preserving options.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** The model answers the wrong question, excludes important consequences, or treats a means/proxy as the end.

**Historical origin.** Early OR's study of operations; decision analysis; problem-structuring methods; risk-informed engineering decisions.

**How the property evolved.** From operational problem definition to value-focused, risk-informed, stakeholder-aware and implementation-aware framing.

**Important criticisms.** Decision analysis can be normatively coherent while elicited preferences are unstable or stakeholders disagree; explicitness does not make values objective.

**Known failure modes.** Omitted alternatives, inherited framing, false precision, authority gaps, post-hoc criteria changes, and a model with no live decision consumer.

**Lineage class.** HYBRID

**Evolved form in this source.** Model choice is downstream of a challengeable decision frame and can be changed without changing the underlying consequence statement.

**Mechanism.** State the decision, consequence, owner, horizon, boundary, affected parties, implementability and what would count as a materially different outcome before formal modelling.

**Model assumptions.** The represented alternatives and consequences span the live decision sufficiently for the claimed conclusion; framing and elicitation are not neutral facts.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A one-page decision frame or direct authoritative constraint can suffice where alternatives are few and consequence is clear.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The valued consequence, decision criteria, hard constraints, soft preferences, and non-compensatory limits must be distinguishable and challengeable.

**Open questions.** How much stakeholder participation is required before a frame is legitimate in distributed systems?

**Original form.** Investigate the operation and objective before constructing a mathematical representation.

**Primary sources.** S006; S040; S047; S071; S072; S073; S075

**Problem it addressed.** A tractable mathematical question is substituted for the consequential operational decision.

**Property id.** P002

**Property name.** Frame the real decision and consequence before selecting a model

**Relationship class.** OPERATIONS_RESEARCH_NATIVE

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** The resources needed to analyse and implement each alternative are included where they can change feasibility or ranking.

**Trigger or context.** Whenever analysis will rank alternatives, allocate scarce resources, or automate action.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Material ambiguity about consequences, preferences, feasibility, timing, or future information is stated rather than silently converted to precise inputs.

**Requalification.** **Claim ids.** C002

**Disposition.** RETAINED_WITH_SCOPE

**Fresh proposition.** Framing must make the action, consequence, horizon and material uncertainty explicit enough to distinguish the alternatives that could change the choice.

**Fresh note.**

**Scope.** Scope is proportionate to consequence; problem-structuring disagreement may precede a numerical model.

**Evaluation.** Ask whether two reasonable frames give materially different feasible choices or valued consequences; expose the difference before selecting a model.

**Witness.** Including shortage cost changes a demand forecast comparison; optimizing an unexplained proxy does not establish the intended consequence.

## P003 — Generate and preserve meaningful alternatives before ranking or optimisation

**Ceremony vs property.** Brainstorming workshops and design-space tools are optional; the generation/evaluation separation is the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S043

**Critical sources.** S098; S099; S046

**Status at this source freeze.** DECISION_FRAME_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Only information capable of discriminating alternatives is required on the cheap path; provenance, timing, and material omissions must be known when formal analysis is used.

**Decision or consumer.** The accountable decision owner and implementers who must act on the result.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** A precise ranking chooses the best member of an impoverished or politically pre-filtered set.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** A named person or body must own the decision, be able to accept consequences, and have a deadline or review point. Analysts and solvers are not authority by default.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S062; S099

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** MODERATE

**Contrary evidence strength.** MODERATE

**Empirical comparative strength.** MODERATE

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** MODERATE

**Historical provenance strength.** MODERATE

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** LOW

**Synthesis note.** Strong practice logic and cross-domain transfer; no general completeness theorem.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Fewer category errors, fewer dominated or infeasible commitments, and a traceable basis for choosing, deferring, escalating, or preserving options.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** A precise ranking chooses the best member of an impoverished or politically pre-filtered set.

**Historical origin.** Value-focused thinking, design-solution definition, analysis of alternatives, exploratory modelling and option design.

**How the property evolved.** From choosing among given acts to value-focused alternative creation, exploratory modelling, portfolios and option-preserving staged commitments.

**Important criticisms.** Decision analysis can be normatively coherent while elicited preferences are unstable or stakeholders disagree; explicitness does not make values objective.

**Known failure modes.** Omitted alternatives, inherited framing, false precision, authority gaps, post-hoc criteria changes, and a model with no live decision consumer.

**Lineage class.** DECISION_ANALYSIS_LINEAGE

**Evolved form in this source.** The decision set is treated as an engineered object with provenance, feasibility screening, dominance checks and a reopen rule.

**Mechanism.** Separate alternative generation from evaluation; use objectives, decomposition, scenarios, design-space exploration and stakeholder/local knowledge to create options, including hybrids and staged choices.

**Model assumptions.** The represented alternatives and consequences span the live decision sufficiently for the claimed conclusion; framing and elicitation are not neutral facts.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** For routine choices, screen a small known set and ask explicitly whether a materially different option exists.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The valued consequence, decision criteria, hard constraints, soft preferences, and non-compensatory limits must be distinguishable and challengeable.

**Open questions.** How can alternative-generation completeness be bounded without pretending the design space is enumerable?

**Original form.** Create alternatives from values and objectives rather than only compare options already supplied.

**Primary sources.** S047; S074; S076; S035

**Problem it addressed.** Optimisation cannot select an alternative, design, policy, or staged option that was never represented.

**Property id.** P003

**Property name.** Generate and preserve meaningful alternatives before ranking or optimisation

**Relationship class.** DECISION_ANALYSIS_NATIVE

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** The resources needed to analyse and implement each alternative are included where they can change feasibility or ranking.

**Trigger or context.** High-consequence, irreversible, novel or underperforming decisions; decisions where all listed options score poorly.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Material ambiguity about consequences, preferences, feasibility, timing, or future information is stated rather than silently converted to precise inputs.

**Requalification.** **Claim ids.** C003

**Fresh proposition.** Compare live feasible alternatives, including newly constructed ones when their plausible value exceeds exploration cost.

**Fresh note.**

**Scope.** Alternatives can actually be chosen within the decision horizon; excluded options have explicit feasibility or relevance reasons.

<a id="p004"></a>

## P004 — Include status quo, no-action, deferment, and staged commitment where feasible

**Ceremony vs property.** A formal real-options valuation is optional; explicit omission or inclusion of delay/reversal is the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S036

**Critical sources.** S043; S045

**Status at this source freeze.** DECISION_FRAME_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Only information capable of discriminating alternatives is required on the cheap path; provenance, timing, and material omissions must be known when formal analysis is used.

**Decision or consumer.** The accountable decision owner and implementers who must act on the result.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** An intervention is labelled optimal only because no-action, delay, rollback, pilot, or staged investment was excluded.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** A named person or body must own the decision, be able to accept consequences, and have a deadline or review point. Analysts and solvers are not authority by default.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S036

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** MODERATE

**Empirical comparative strength.** LOW

**Field or operational strength.** MODERATE

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** MODERATE

**Implementation case strength.** MODERATE

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** LOW

**Synthesis note.** Strong option-theoretic support; valuation is assumption-sensitive.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Fewer category errors, fewer dominated or infeasible commitments, and a traceable basis for choosing, deferring, escalating, or preserving options.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** An intervention is labelled optimal only because no-action, delay, rollback, pilot, or staged investment was excluded.

**Historical origin.** Decision trees, real-options analysis, risk-informed decision making and formal analyses of alternatives.

**How the property evolved.** Static no-action comparison evolved into real-option and staged-commitment treatment of learning and irreversibility.

**Important criticisms.** Decision analysis can be normatively coherent while elicited preferences are unstable or stakeholders disagree; explicitness does not make values objective.

**Known failure modes.** Omitted alternatives, inherited framing, false precision, authority gaps, post-hoc criteria changes, and a model with no live decision consumer.

**Lineage class.** HYBRID

**Evolved form in this source.** The choice set includes feasible inaction and timing policies, with their risks and foregone benefits, without presuming delay is valuable.

**Mechanism.** Represent baseline consequences, opportunity costs, information arrival, reversibility, expiry and staged gates as alternatives when they are physically and institutionally possible.

**Model assumptions.** The represented alternatives and consequences span the live decision sufficiently for the claimed conclusion; framing and elicitation are not neutral facts.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** For urgent or mandatory action, record why no-action/deferment is infeasible instead of modelling it.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The valued consequence, decision criteria, hard constraints, soft preferences, and non-compensatory limits must be distinguishable and challengeable.

**Open questions.** When do organisational commitments make a nominal no-action option infeasible in practice?

**Original form.** Compare active interventions with not acting now, waiting for information, or preserving flexibility.

**Primary sources.** S035; S036; S040; S075; S076

**Problem it addressed.** Action bias or programme framing omits the baseline and forces selection among interventions.

**Property id.** P004

**Property name.** Include status quo, no-action, deferment, and staged commitment where feasible

**Relationship class.** ECONOMICS_OR_UTILITY_IMPORT

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** The resources needed to analyse and implement each alternative are included where they can change feasibility or ranking.

**Trigger or context.** Irreversible, capital-intensive, uncertain, novel, or rapidly changing decisions.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Material ambiguity about consequences, preferences, feasibility, timing, or future information is stated rather than silently converted to precise inputs.

**Requalification.** **Claim ids.** C004

**Fresh proposition.** No action, deferment and information acquisition are alternatives when they are feasible; their delay, default and irreversibility consequences belong in the comparison.

**Fresh note.**

**Scope.** A default is not assumed costless, safe or indefinitely available.

**Evaluation.** Evaluate what actually happens if no commitment occurs before the deadline, including lost options and required actions.

**Witness.** Keeping the current plan can avoid a switching loss; if its resource has disappeared, mere deferment is not a feasible substitute.

## P005 — Distinguish feasible alternatives, enforceable hard constraints, and soft preferences

**Ceremony vs property.** A solver constraint row is not the property; truthful admissibility and enforceability are.

**Contrary evidence.** S099

**Critical sources.** S070; S098; S101

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** The solver returns a mathematically feasible but operationally impossible recommendation, or trades through a genuinely hard limit via a penalty.

**Historical origin.** Linear and integer programming, analysis of alternatives, constrained scheduling and current engineering trade-study guidance.

**How the property evolved.** Pure algebraic feasibility evolved toward mixed hard/soft, chance/robust, implementation and policy constraints with explicit slack semantics.

**Important criticisms.** Decision analysis can be normatively coherent while elicited preferences are unstable or stakeholders disagree; explicitness does not make values objective.

**Known failure modes.** Omitted alternatives, inherited framing, false precision, authority gaps, post-hoc criteria changes, and a model with no live decision consumer.

**Lineage class.** MATHEMATICAL_PROGRAMMING_LINEAGE

**Evolved form in this source.** Constraint status is challengeable and linked to enforcement, uncertainty, recourse and violation consequence; infeasibility is diagnostic, not proof that reality is impossible.

**Mechanism.** Classify constraints by source, owner, enforceability, uncertainty and violation consequence; test feasibility against implementation conditions and preserve veto/lexicographic limits where compensation is illegitimate.

**Model assumptions.** The represented alternatives and consequences span the live decision sufficiently for the claimed conclusion; framing and elicitation are not neutral facts.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use direct admissibility screening before scoring; a hard authoritative discriminator can end the comparison.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The valued consequence, decision criteria, hard constraints, soft preferences, and non-compensatory limits must be distinguishable and challengeable.

**Open questions.** How should contested legal, ethical or policy constraints be represented without laundering them into coefficients?

**Original form.** Define a feasible region with resource and technical constraints; use integrality where actions are discrete.

## P006 — Trace objectives to consequences and resist proxy substitution

**Ceremony vs property.** An objective function or dashboard is optional; objective–consequence validity is the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S106; S107; S108

**Critical sources.** S089; S110; S096; S097

**Status at this source freeze.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Metric provenance, denominators, missingness, threshold behaviour, classification changes, and outcome measures independent of the target are monitored.

**Decision or consumer.** The consequence owner, affected stakeholders, and governance authority—not only the metric-producing unit.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Metric improvement is reported as real improvement despite externalities, displaced cost, hidden queues, selection effects or changed behaviour.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** The owner of the real consequence must be distinct from owners rewarded on the proxy, with authority to audit and revise measures.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S105; S124

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** HIGH

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** MODERATE

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** HIGH

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** LOW

**Synthesis note.** One of the strongest surviving anti-failure properties.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Lower probability that optimisation improves the dashboard while degrading the system or shifting harm outside the model boundary.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Metric improvement is reported as real improvement despite externalities, displaced cost, hidden queues, selection effects or changed behaviour.

**Historical origin.** Operational objectives in early OR; utility/loss modelling; Goodhart/Campbell critiques; modern algorithmic-bias and model-governance evidence.

**How the property evolved.** Single operational objectives evolved toward multi-objective, fairness/externality constraints, causal monitoring and performative-feedback awareness.

**Important criticisms.** Anti-gaming controls themselves can create complexity and new gaming surfaces; the mature form prefers direct consequences and plural indicators where feasible.

**Known failure modes.** Threshold gaming, queue hiding, backlog reclassification, schedule padding, cost shifting, dropped constraints, selective scenarios, and post-hoc weights.

**Lineage class.** CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

**Evolved form in this source.** A proxy remains provisional evidence about a consequence, with independent outcome validation and an explicit gaming/feedback model.

**Mechanism.** Create a consequence-to-objective trace, identify proxy gaps and affected parties, monitor independent outcomes and distribution shifts, and allow retirement or redesign of captured metrics.

**Model assumptions.** The proxy remains causally and institutionally linked to the valued outcome after it becomes a target.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Where a direct outcome or authoritative constraint is observable, use it instead of a composite proxy.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Proxy, target, constraint, distributional impact, externalities, and opportunities for reclassification or cost shifting are explicit.

**Open questions.** What independent outcomes remain observable after optimisation changes the data-generating process?

**Original form.** Optimise an explicit operational objective or expected utility.

**Primary sources.** S006; S070; S105; S106; S107; S108

**Problem it addressed.** A measurable target replaces the actual outcome and changes behaviour once optimised.

**Property id.** P006

**Property name.** Trace objectives to consequences and resist proxy substitution

**Relationship class.** CONVERGENT_PROPERTY

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Independent outcome measurement, audit capacity, metric rotation or retirement, and exception review are funded.

**Trigger or context.** Whenever a KPI, score, forecast loss, utilisation, throughput, service level, or learned reward controls incentives or action.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Strategic adaptation and feedback-induced distribution shift are expected rather than treated as noise.

**Fresh proposition.** Check the link from an optimized metric to the valued consequence, including incentive conflicts, measurement error and unequal meaning across affected groups.

**Fresh note.** P006 authority clause requires distinct owners universally. Replace that necessity with consequence checking and conflict controls proportionate to the decision; the core proxy warning remains sound.

**Scope.** A metric is being used as a substitute; conflict controls may be independent review, triangulation or accountability rather than necessarily distinct people.

**Evaluation.** Compare metric improvement with consequence improvement and inspect who can manipulate either; revise the proxy if the relationship fails.

**Witness.** Health expenditure can understate need for a group with lower access; an accurately predicted cost is then an inaccurate need proxy.

## P007 — Preserve multiple objectives, Pareto structure, and non-compensatory limits

**Ceremony vs property.** A weighted matrix is optional and often inferior; explicit trade-offs and admissibility are the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S078; S079; S080

**Critical sources.** S078; S079; S080

**Status at this source freeze.** MULTI_CRITERIA_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Alternative performance evidence, scale definitions, normalisation rules, weights or preference statements, and uncertainty ranges are traceable.

**Decision or consumer.** The accountable owner and stakeholders whose values are represented or whose disagreement is preserved.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Aggregate score improves while a critical criterion, stakeholder, tail consequence or hard threshold worsens unacceptably.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** A legitimate decision owner and affected stakeholders must own criteria, thresholds, weights, vetoes, and aggregation rules.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S083

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** MODERATE

**Field or operational strength.** MODERATE

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** MODERATE

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** LOW

**Synthesis note.** Strong formal support; aggregation methods remain preference- and assumption-sensitive.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Transparent, challengeable trade-offs and preservation of Pareto, veto, and stakeholder information that a single proxy would erase.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Aggregate score improves while a critical criterion, stakeholder, tail consequence or hard threshold worsens unacceptably.

**Historical origin.** Multi-attribute utility, vector optimisation, Pareto efficiency, outranking and modern multi-objective optimisation.

**How the property evolved.** Weighted utility evolved into Pareto sets, outranking, non-compensatory rules, interactive elicitation and sensitivity analysis.

**Important criticisms.** Different MCDA methods embody different value models and can rank the same alternatives differently; explicit judgement is not eliminated by arithmetic.

**Known failure modes.** Rank reversal, arbitrary normalisation, false cardinal arithmetic on ordinal scores, compensating across hard limits, post-hoc weights, and spreadsheet objectivity theatre.

**Lineage class.** MULTI_CRITERIA_DECISION_LINEAGE

**Evolved form in this source.** Conflict remains visible; aggregation is an owned preference model, not objective truth, and alternative rankings are tested across plausible preferences.

**Mechanism.** Use dominance/Pareto screening, thresholds/vetoes or lexicographic rules, expose scalarisation assumptions, and show efficient/near-efficient alternatives rather than only one score.

**Model assumptions.** Preferential/utility independence, cardinality, compensability, commensurability, and aggregation-method assumptions are explicit.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use a small consequence table and dominance check; do not aggregate when one alternative clearly dominates or a hard threshold decides.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Criteria meanings, scale type, non-compensatory limits, stakeholder conflicts, and uncertainty are explicit; a total score is not presumed to be value truth.

**Open questions.** How should irreducible stakeholder disagreement be represented when no legitimate common utility exists?

**Original form.** Represent several valued attributes and their preference trade-offs or efficient frontier.

**Primary sources.** S041; S077; S081; S082; S083; S109

**Problem it addressed.** A single weighted objective hides conflict, permits illegitimate compensation, or presents one preference choice as mathematically necessary.

**Property id.** P007

**Property name.** Preserve multiple objectives, Pareto structure, and non-compensatory limits

**Relationship class.** HYBRID_RESOLUTION

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Elicitation, facilitation, dispute resolution, data collection, and sensitivity analysis costs are proportionate to consequence.

**Trigger or context.** Decisions with genuinely conflicting consequences, stakeholder values, quality attributes or fairness/efficiency tensions.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Weight, score, threshold, normalisation, and alternative-set sensitivity are analysed where they could change ranking or admissibility.

**Fresh proposition.** Represent material multiple objectives and admissible tradeoffs; additive weighting is one conditional representation and is not intrinsically inferior.

**Fresh note.** The broad often-inferior comparison is not newly established by the inspected evidence. Conditional additive validity does not refute a defined comparative-frequency claim; the fresh assessment retains conditional suitability and rejection of compulsory matrices.

**Scope.** Criteria, scales, preference independence and compensation rules suit the decision; irreducible disagreement is retained.

**Contrary evidence.** S036

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Only information capable of discriminating alternatives is required on the cheap path; provenance, timing, and material omissions must be known when formal analysis is used.

**Decision or consumer.** The accountable decision owner and implementers who must act on the result.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** An irreversible action is taken too early, or a reversible low-cost experiment is over-analysed as though it were final commitment.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** A named person or body must own the decision, be able to accept consequences, and have a deadline or review point. Analysts and solvers are not authority by default.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** An irreversible action is taken too early, or a reversible low-cost experiment is over-analysed as though it were final commitment.

**Historical origin.** Sequential decision theory, real options, investment under uncertainty and risk-informed engineering decisions.

**How the property evolved.** Financial real-options models broadened into staged experiments, pilots, modular commitments and explicit rollback/exit criteria.

**Important criticisms.** Decision analysis can be normatively coherent while elicited preferences are unstable or stakeholders disagree; explicitness does not make values objective.

**Known failure modes.** Omitted alternatives, inherited framing, false precision, authority gaps, post-hoc criteria changes, and a model with no live decision consumer.

**Lineage class.** HYBRID

**Evolved form in this source.** Decision depth and commitment scale are proportional to irreversibility; reversible options can be exercised cheaply while irreversible choices preserve learning value.

**Mechanism.** Classify reversibility, lock-in, expiry, switching/rollback cost, learning opportunities and staged gates; price formally only where inputs support it.

**Model assumptions.** The represented alternatives and consequences span the live decision sufficiently for the claimed conclusion; framing and elicitation are not neutral facts.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use qualitative option-preservation and reversibility screening for small or poorly quantified decisions.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The valued consequence, decision criteria, hard constraints, soft preferences, and non-compensatory limits must be distinguishable and challengeable.

**Open questions.** How should option value be bounded where future alternatives are endogenous and probabilities are unidentified?

**Original form.** Delay or stage irreversible investment when future information and flexibility have value.

**Primary sources.** S035; S036; S039; S075

**Problem it addressed.** Expected-value comparison treats timing and commitment as irrelevant and can destroy valuable future choices.

**How the property evolved.** Finite-stage dynamic programming evolved toward MDPs, approximate/online methods, receding horizons and policy-based operation.

**Important criticisms.** Decision analysis can be normatively coherent while elicited preferences are unstable or stakeholders disagree; explicitness does not make values objective.

**Known failure modes.** Omitted alternatives, inherited framing, false precision, authority gaps, post-hoc criteria changes, and a model with no live decision consumer.

**Lineage class.** SEQUENTIAL_OR_ONLINE_DECISION_LINEAGE

**Evolved form in this source.** The unit of decision is a state-contingent policy or staged commitment when future coupling matters, not a falsely independent one-shot optimum.

**Mechanism.** Identify state, stage, dependency, future information, transition, terminal consequence and commitment; use a simple look-ahead or formal dynamic model as warranted.

**Model assumptions.** The represented alternatives and consequences span the live decision sufficiently for the claimed conclusion; framing and elicitation are not neutral facts.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use an explicit two-step consequence/dependency check when full dynamic programming is disproportionate.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The valued consequence, decision criteria, hard constraints, soft preferences, and non-compensatory limits must be distinguishable and challengeable.

**Open questions.** What horizon is sufficient when distant consequences are uncertain but potentially irreversible?

**Original form.** Choose a policy over states and stages rather than an isolated action.

**Primary sources.** S004; S011; S012; S013; S035

**Problem it addressed.** A locally attractive action changes future state, information, capacity, or available alternatives.

**Property id.** P010

**Property name.** Represent decision horizon, dependencies, and sequential coupling

**Relationship class.** CONTROL_THEORY_IMPORT_OR_SHARED_ANCESTRY

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** The resources needed to analyse and implement each alternative are included where they can change feasibility or ranking.

**Trigger or context.** Linked decisions, replenishment, scheduling, capacity investment, maintenance, adaptive policies and any action with material carry-over effects.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Material ambiguity about consequences, preferences, feasibility, timing, or future information is stated rather than silently converted to precise inputs.

**Requalification.** **Claim ids.** C010

**Fresh note.**

## P011 — Match model fidelity and analysis depth to decision consequence

**Ceremony vs property.** A comprehensive model is not inherently mature; decision-relative adequacy is.

**Contrary evidence.** S048; S089

**Critical sources.** S089; S098; S101

**Status at this source freeze.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Data or measurement preconditions.** An estimate—formal or qualitative—of whether another observation, model refinement, scenario, review, or computation could change action.

**Decision or consumer.** The decision owner who can stop, continue, reopen, or act.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Analysis costs and delays grow while added fidelity cannot change the next action, or a crude model is used where small errors reverse an irreversible choice.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** The decision owner must set the value of delay, error, information, computation, and reversibility; the analyst cannot optimise analysis effort against an unstated consequence model.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S101

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** MODERATE

**Contrary evidence strength.** MODERATE

**Empirical comparative strength.** MODERATE

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** LOW

**Synthesis note.** Strong cross-lineage convergence; exact adequacy threshold remains context-owned.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** More timely decisions and concentration of scarce analysis effort where it can plausibly alter consequences.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Analysis costs and delays grow while added fidelity cannot change the next action, or a crude model is used where small errors reverse an irreversible choice.

**Historical origin.** Requisite decision modelling, bounded rationality/metareasoning, model-validation practice and NASA decision guidance.

**How the property evolved.** Bounded rationality and requisite models converged with value-of-information, validation, and risk-proportionate engineering guidance.

**Important criticisms.** Exact value-of-information and metareasoning models are assumption-intensive; a qualitative decision-change test is often the mature cheap path.

**Known failure modes.** Analysis paralysis, arbitrary deadlines, stopping on fatigue, endless scenario accumulation, or over-formal VOI built on invented probabilities.

**Lineage class.** CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

**Evolved form in this source.** Use the least-cost model that is adequate to discriminate the consequential decision, with a reopen rule when state or sensitivity changes.

**Mechanism.** Link fidelity to consequence, uncertainty, reversibility and decision sensitivity; test whether refinements can change admissibility or ranking.

**Model assumptions.** The current alternative ordering and plausible information effects are represented well enough to judge marginal decision value.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use bounds, dominance, sensitivity or a simple discriminator before building a richer model.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The analysis objective is decision improvement, not model detail, run count, document completeness, or solver prestige.

**Open questions.** How should adequacy be tested when several stakeholders have different loss functions?

**Original form.** Build only enough model detail for the decision; a model is requisite when further refinement does not materially alter choice.

**Primary sources.** S043; S048; S049; S050; S073; S075

**Problem it addressed.** Detail, precision and integration are mistaken for decision quality.

**Property id.** P011

**Property name.** Match model fidelity and analysis depth to decision consequence

**Relationship class.** HYBRID_RESOLUTION

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Time, attention, compute, experiment cost, and opportunity cost are explicit analysis resources.

**Trigger or context.** Any choice of modelling depth, simulation detail, data collection, solver exactness or review breadth.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Uncertainty in both the underlying decision and the analysis-value estimate is acknowledged; formal VOI formulas require credible probability and value models.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** The decision owner must set the value of delay, error, information, computation, and reversibility; the analyst cannot optimise analysis effort against an unstated consequence model.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Model assumptions.** The current alternative ordering and plausible information effects are represented well enough to judge marginal decision value.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The analysis objective is decision improvement, not model detail, run count, document completeness, or solver prestige.

**Open questions.** How should qualitative VOI be governed when probabilities, utilities or model classes are disputed?

**Original form.** Expected value of perfect/sample information and computation compares improved decisions with acquisition cost.

## P015 — State model assumptions and validity domain as part of the decision claim

**Ceremony vs property.** A generic limitations section is insufficient; assumptions must be linked to decision sensitivity.

**Contrary evidence.** S088; S089

**Critical sources.** S070; S098; S110

**Status at this source freeze.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Coefficients, feasible sets, capacities, costs, forecasts, and current state have provenance and uncertainty appropriate to their leverage on the solution.

**Decision or consumer.** The owner of the allocation, design, policy, or schedule and the operators who must implement it.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** A valid calculation is transferred outside its stationarity, independence, causal, behavioural, distributional, or implementation domain.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** The decision owner must approve the represented objective, constraints, alternatives, tolerances, and implementation rules; solver output is advice unless authority is explicitly automated.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S087; S105

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** HIGH

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** HIGH

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** HIGH

**Synthesis note.** Extremely strong cross-domain support.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Transparent resource trade-offs and high-value improvements in complex feasible spaces where simpler discrimination is inadequate.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** A valid calculation is transferred outside its stationarity, independence, causal, behavioural, distributional, or implementation domain.

**Historical origin.** Mathematical programming, queueing, simulation validation, stochastic/robust optimisation and model-criticism traditions.

**How the property evolved.** Method-specific assumptions evolved into model-risk, validation-domain, uncertainty-set and out-of-sample governance.

**Important criticisms.** Formal optimality is conditional on the model; small optimality gaps can coexist with large real-world regret, and more exact algorithms do not repair misspecification.

**Known failure modes.** Optimising the wrong objective, infeasible or unenforceable constraints, numerical false precision, brittle point estimates, local optimum/global harm, and solver optimality worship.

**Lineage class.** CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

**Evolved form in this source.** Claims are scoped: optimal/valid under a stated representation and current evidence, with explicit transfer and drift limits.

**Mechanism.** Attach an assumption register and validity boundary to each material recommendation; test high-leverage assumptions through sensitivity, validation, stress or field evidence.

**Model assumptions.** Functional form, linearity/convexity, integrality, independence, horizon, decomposition, and numerical tolerances are stated and tested where material.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** List only assumptions capable of changing action; use a direct caveat and fallback when validation is infeasible.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The mathematical objective is traceable to valued consequences; hard constraints are enforceable; penalties, scaling, and proxies are challengeable.

**Open questions.** Which model assumptions require empirical validation versus merely transparent disclosure?

**Original form.** Mathematical conclusions are conditional on model axioms, distributions, feasible sets and data.

**Primary sources.** S007; S027; S028; S084; S088; S089

**Problem it addressed.** A theorem or solver result is presented without the assumptions that make it relevant to the real operation.

**Property id.** P015

**Property name.** State model assumptions and validity domain as part of the decision claim

**Relationship class.** CONVERGENT_PROPERTY

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Formulation, solve time, compute, data maintenance, implementation effort, and switching/reconfiguration costs are part of the decision.

**Trigger or context.** Any formal model, simulation, forecast, queue formula, scorecard, optimisation or learned policy.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Sensitivity, out-of-sample performance, scenario or ambiguity analysis, and model-form uncertainty are addressed according to consequence.

**Requalification.** **Claim ids.** C015

## P016 — Test sensitivity to parameters, probabilities, weights, and modelling choices

**Ceremony vs property.** A tornado chart is optional; exposing decision fragility is the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S070; S089

**Critical sources.** S078; S080; S089

**Status at this source freeze.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Coefficients, feasible sets, capacities, costs, forecasts, and current state have provenance and uncertainty appropriate to their leverage on the solution.

**Decision or consumer.** The owner of the allocation, design, policy, or schedule and the operators who must implement it.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Small estimation, elicitation or normalisation changes reverse ranking, feasibility, priority or resource allocation.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** The decision owner must approve the represented objective, constraints, alternatives, tolerances, and implementation rules; solver output is advice unless authority is explicitly automated.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S083; S087

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** MODERATE

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** HIGH

**Synthesis note.** Strong retained property; ranges remain judgment-dependent.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Transparent resource trade-offs and high-value improvements in complex feasible spaces where simpler discrimination is inadequate.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Small estimation, elicitation or normalisation changes reverse ranking, feasibility, priority or resource allocation.

**Historical origin.** Linear-programming sensitivity/duality, decision analysis, MCDA, robust optimisation and model-validation practice.

**How the property evolved.** Local coefficient ranges broadened into global sensitivity, scenario/stress analysis, robustness sets, rank sensitivity and out-of-sample evaluation.

**Important criticisms.** Formal optimality is conditional on the model; small optimality gaps can coexist with large real-world regret, and more exact algorithms do not repair misspecification.

**Known failure modes.** Optimising the wrong objective, infeasible or unenforceable constraints, numerical false precision, brittle point estimates, local optimum/global harm, and solver optimality worship.

**Lineage class.** HYBRID

**Evolved form in this source.** Sensitivity is tied to whether action changes, not merely to output variance; unstable decisions trigger more information, robust options or preserved alternatives.

**Mechanism.** Identify high-leverage inputs; vary plausible ranges and alternative model forms; report decision regions, breakpoints, rank changes, near-optimal alternatives and unresolved sensitivities.

**Model assumptions.** Functional form, linearity/convexity, integrality, independence, horizon, decomposition, and numerical tolerances are stated and tested where material.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use one-way break-even and dominance tests before global or probabilistic sensitivity analysis.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The mathematical objective is traceable to valued consequences; hard constraints are enforceable; penalties, scaling, and proxies are challengeable.

**Open questions.** How should correlated model-form choices be explored without combinatorial scenario theatre?

**Original form.** Examine how solution or value changes with coefficients and assumptions.

**Primary sources.** S007; S025; S040; S041; S073; S083

**Problem it addressed.** A recommendation appears precise but is unstable to plausible changes in inputs or modelling conventions.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Sensitivity, out-of-sample performance, scenario or ambiguity analysis, and model-form uncertainty are addressed according to consequence.

## P019 — Establish implementation feasibility, operational authority, and usable decision support

**Ceremony vs property.** Change-management activity is not inherently required; operational feasibility and authority are.

**Contrary evidence.** S104; S100

**Critical sources.** S098; S105

**Status at this source freeze.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Live data freshness, local constraints, exceptions, incentives, override behaviour, and post-decision outcomes are observable.

**Decision or consumer.** The people or automated systems that act, plus the authority that owns exceptions and consequences.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** The recommendation is ignored, selectively followed, worked around, or causes hidden operational damage.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** The operational authority, data owner, model owner, affected users, and override/escalation rights must be explicit.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S100; S104; S124

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** MODERATE

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** HIGH

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** LOW

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** LOW

**Synthesis note.** Strong field and case support; mechanisms vary by organisation.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Recommendations that survive contact with authority, workflow, incentives, data latency, and operator knowledge.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** The recommendation is ignored, selectively followed, worked around, or causes hidden operational damage.

**Historical origin.** OR implementation studies, problem-structuring methods, decision conferencing, engineering trade guidance and human–algorithm research.

**How the property evolved.** ‘User acceptance’ framing evolved toward sociotechnical fit, local-knowledge feedback, trust calibration, override governance and model ownership.

**Important criticisms.** Adoption is not proof of validity and resistance is not proof of irrationality; implementation can reveal missing constraints or conflicting objectives.

**Known failure modes.** Technically superior but unusable recommendations, shadow systems, stale models, selective compliance, unowned overrides, and blaming users for revealed model defects.

**Lineage class.** HYBRID

**Evolved form in this source.** Implementation is part of model validity; resistance can be evidence of missing constraints rather than a defect in users.

**Mechanism.** Co-design constraints and exceptions with operators, identify decision/override rights, test workflow and data latency, expose sensitivities, plan fallback, and measure implementation costs.

**Model assumptions.** Operators can execute the recommendation, incentives do not systematically defeat it, and the model remains current after deployment.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** For one-off simple decisions, verify authority, resource availability and direct executability with the responsible operator.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The implementable consequence, not mathematical recommendation alone, is the success criterion.

**Open questions.** How should override rights be calibrated so that local knowledge is preserved without defeating valid constraints?

**Original form.** Deliver recommendations to managers/operators for implementation.

**Primary sources.** S073; S075; S076; S099; S100; S101; S102; S104

**Problem it addressed.** The mathematically preferred action conflicts with authority, workflow, incentives, local knowledge, explanation needs or integration constraints.

**Property id.** P019

**Property name.** Establish implementation feasibility, operational authority, and usable decision support

**Relationship class.** INDUSTRIAL_ENGINEERING_HYBRID

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Training, integration, change effort, exception handling, maintenance, and feedback capacity are included.

**Trigger or context.** Any recommendation requiring organisational behaviour, software integration, recurring operator use, or automated enforcement.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Human adaptation, workarounds, changing incentives, trust calibration, and organisational drift are treated as model uncertainty.

## P021 — Represent demand, arrivals, service, capacity, and variability before capacity claims

**Ceremony vs property.** M/M/1 notation is optional; representing demand, service and variability is not.

**Contrary evidence.** S055; S056

**Critical sources.** S054; S055; S057

**Status at this source freeze.** QUEUE_CAPACITY_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Arrival/demand timing, service-time or capacity behaviour, variability, retries, blocking, priority classes, and backlog/WIP measurements at decision-relevant granularity.

**Decision or consumer.** The owner of staffing, capacity, admission, prioritisation, or service-level policy.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Average capacity appears sufficient but bursts or service variability produce long queues, loss, abandonment or missed deadlines.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** An operational owner must control or influence capacity, admission, priority, service discipline, demand shaping, or service commitments.

## P022 — Govern utilisation jointly with waiting, congestion, and tail performance

**Ceremony vs property.** A fixed ‘safe utilisation’ percentage is not general; the utilisation–delay trade-off is.

**Contrary evidence.** S055

**Critical sources.** S052; S055; S056

**Status at this source freeze.** QUEUE_CAPACITY_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Arrival/demand timing, service-time or capacity behaviour, variability, retries, blocking, priority classes, and backlog/WIP measurements at decision-relevant granularity.

**Decision or consumer.** The owner of staffing, capacity, admission, prioritisation, or service-level policy.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Near-100% utilisation creates exploding delay, tail latency, abandonment, brittle recovery and hidden backlog.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** An operational owner must control or influence capacity, admission, priority, service discipline, demand shaping, or service commitments.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S053; S054

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** MODERATE

**Empirical comparative strength.** HIGH

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** HIGH

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** HIGH

**Synthesis note.** Among the strongest queueing-derived properties.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Avoided congestion collapse, credible service commitments, visible bottlenecks, and capacity decisions that account for variability and delay.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Near-100% utilisation creates exploding delay, tail latency, abandonment, brittle recovery and hidden backlog.

**Historical origin.** Heavy-traffic queueing, many-server staffing, service operations, Factory Physics and large-scale computer systems.

**How the property evolved.** Mean waiting formulas evolved into heavy-traffic, quality-and-efficiency-driven, tail-at-scale and time-varying capacity policies.

**Important criticisms.** Parametric queue models can be powerful within domain but are brittle under heterogeneous, strategic, nonstationary, or feedback-coupled work; qualitative congestion properties transfer more widely than distributions.

**Known failure modes.** Average-rate fallacy, hidden queues, 100% utilisation targets, steady-state formulas in transient overload, false independence, and adding parallel workers behind a shared bottleneck.

**Lineage class.** QUEUEING_AND_TELETRAFFIC_LINEAGE

**Evolved form in this source.** Efficiency is assessed at system throughput and service consequence, not maximum busy fraction; controlled idle capacity can be rational.

**Mechanism.** Set utilisation/capacity targets from acceptable waiting/tail/service consequences and variability; retain reserve where marginal delay cost justifies it.

**Model assumptions.** Stationarity, independence, service discipline, routing, finite/infinite buffers, steady-state existence, and workload homogeneity are explicit where formulas rely on them.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Plot utilisation, queue age and tail delay together; reduce load or add reserve when congestion inflects.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Throughput, waiting, abandonment, service level, tail latency, cost, fairness, and overload consequences must not be collapsed into utilisation alone.

**Open questions.** How should tail and fairness costs be combined when capacity serves heterogeneous classes?

**Original form.** Waiting grows nonlinearly as offered load approaches service capacity under queueing assumptions.

**Primary sources.** S016; S051; S053; S054; S057

**Problem it addressed.** Utilisation is optimised as if idle capacity were pure waste.

**Property id.** P022

**Property name.** Govern utilisation jointly with waiting, congestion, and tail performance

**Fresh proposition.** In specified stochastic queue regimes, high utilization can sharply increase delay; the exact relation and feasible service target are model dependent.

**Fresh note.** Retain congestion mechanism; avoid replacing it with a universal queue formula or threshold.

**Scope.** The selected service discipline, stability, arrival/service assumptions and bottleneck boundary are satisfied.

**Evaluation.** Compare observed delay and workload variability with an admitted model; do not infer an M/M/1 value from mean utilization alone.

**Witness.** At rho=.9, M/M/1 sojourn10 contrasts with deterministic service/arrival sojourn1; no universal numeric utilization threshold follows.

## P023 — Make queues, WIP, backlogs, blocking, and ageing visible

**Ceremony vs property.** A Kanban board or queue dashboard is optional; complete work-state visibility is the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S106; S107

**Critical sources.** S054; S055; S106; S107

**Status at this source freeze.** QUEUE_CAPACITY_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Arrival/demand timing, service-time or capacity behaviour, variability, retries, blocking, priority classes, and backlog/WIP measurements at decision-relevant granularity.

**Decision or consumer.** The owner of staffing, capacity, admission, prioritisation, or service-level policy.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Reported utilisation or completion metrics look healthy while work ages in hidden queues, blocked states, retries or handoffs.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** An operational owner must control or influence capacity, admission, priority, service discipline, demand shaping, or service commitments.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S054; S056

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** MODERATE

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** HIGH

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** HIGH

**Synthesis note.** Strong conservation/visibility property; steady-state formula use must be bounded.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Avoided congestion collapse, credible service commitments, visible bottlenecks, and capacity decisions that account for variability and delay.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Reported utilisation or completion metrics look healthy while work ages in hidden queues, blocked states, retries or handoffs.

**Historical origin.** Little's Law, queueing networks, production-flow and service-operations practice.

**How the property evolved.** Single queue counts broadened into network flow, WIP-age distributions, retry queues, observability and end-to-end latency.

**Important criticisms.** Parametric queue models can be powerful within domain but are brittle under heterogeneous, strategic, nonstationary, or feedback-coupled work; qualitative congestion properties transfer more widely than distributions.

**Known failure modes.** Average-rate fallacy, hidden queues, 100% utilisation targets, steady-state formulas in transient overload, false independence, and adding parallel workers behind a shared bottleneck.

**Lineage class.** QUEUEING_AND_TELETRAFFIC_LINEAGE

**Evolved form in this source.** Waiting work is a first-class system state with explicit ownership, ageing and downstream consequence; dashboards cannot erase queues by classification.

**Mechanism.** Define queue/WIP states and boundaries; measure arrivals, departures, age, blocked/retry work and flow conservation; reconcile hidden work with throughput and cycle time.

**Model assumptions.** Stationarity, independence, service discipline, routing, finite/infinite buffers, steady-state existence, and workload homogeneity are explicit where formulas rely on them.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Count current WIP/backlog by age and stage; apply flow conservation/Little's Law only after boundary and stability checks.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Throughput, waiting, abandonment, service level, tail latency, cost, fairness, and overload consequences must not be collapsed into utilisation alone.

**Open questions.** How should partially observed or strategically hidden queues be estimated?

**Original form.** Relate average number in system, throughput/arrival rate and time in system under stable flow.

**Primary sources.** S014; S015; S057

**Problem it addressed.** Work waiting between stages is absent from dashboards or reclassified, so delay and overload are invisible.

**Property id.** P023

**Fresh proposition.** Matched-flow occupancy accounting relates work in process, throughput and time; long-run and finite-window forms have distinct boundary conditions.

**Fresh note.** The identity is not restricted universally to Poisson, exponential or stationary queues.

**Scope.** All quantities use the same population, time unit and system boundary; finite windows include partial residence or boundary corrections.

## P024 — Identify system bottlenecks and optimise end-to-end flow rather than local utilisation

**Ceremony vs property.** A bottleneck chart is optional; system-boundary flow reasoning is the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S098

**Critical sources.** S054; S055; S098

**Status at this source freeze.** QUEUE_CAPACITY_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Arrival/demand timing, service-time or capacity behaviour, variability, retries, blocking, priority classes, and backlog/WIP measurements at decision-relevant granularity.

**Decision or consumer.** The owner of staffing, capacity, admission, prioritisation, or service-level policy.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Local optimisation creates excess WIP, starvation, blocking, retries or cost without increasing end-to-end throughput or consequence.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** An operational owner must control or influence capacity, admission, priority, service discipline, demand shaping, or service commitments.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Avoided congestion collapse, credible service commitments, visible bottlenecks, and capacity decisions that account for variability and delay.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Local optimisation creates excess WIP, starvation, blocking, retries or cost without increasing end-to-end throughput or consequence.

**Historical origin.** Network flow, queueing networks, scheduling bounds and production-flow traditions.

**How the property evolved.** Static capacity cuts and queue networks evolved into dynamic bottleneck, network-control and system-level performance governance.

**Important criticisms.** Parametric queue models can be powerful within domain but are brittle under heterogeneous, strategic, nonstationary, or feedback-coupled work; qualitative congestion properties transfer more widely than distributions.

**Known failure modes.** Average-rate fallacy, hidden queues, 100% utilisation targets, steady-state formulas in transient overload, false independence, and adding parallel workers behind a shared bottleneck.

**Lineage class.** HYBRID

**Evolved form in this source.** Capacity action is justified by marginal end-to-end consequence and accounts for bottleneck migration after intervention.

**Mechanism.** Map flow and constraints end to end; identify marginal/system bottlenecks, shared acceptance channels and feedback; subordinate local targets to global consequence.

**Model assumptions.** Stationarity, independence, service discipline, routing, finite/infinite buffers, steady-state existence, and workload homogeneity are explicit where formulas rely on them.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Compare arrival/completion rates and queue age by stage; test whether adding local capacity changes end-to-end flow.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Throughput, waiting, abandonment, service level, tail latency, cost, fairness, and overload consequences must not be collapsed into utilisation alone.

**Open questions.** How should moving and endogenous bottlenecks be governed under adaptive demand?

**Original form.** Capacity constraints limit network flow; coupled service stations propagate congestion.

**Primary sources.** S009; S014; S017; S057

**Problem it addressed.** Each local resource is made busy or locally efficient even when system output is limited elsewhere.

## P025 — Size capacity reserve and slack from variability, consequence, recovery, and coupling

**Ceremony vs property.** A universal buffer percentage is rejected; consequence-linked reserve is the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S033; S058

**Critical sources.** S033; S055

**Status at this source freeze.** QUEUE_CAPACITY_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Arrival/demand timing, service-time or capacity behaviour, variability, retries, blocking, priority classes, and backlog/WIP measurements at decision-relevant granularity.

**Decision or consumer.** The owner of staffing, capacity, admission, prioritisation, or service-level policy.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** System cannot absorb surge, failure, rework or recovery; or excess reserve imposes unjustified cost and hides problems.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** An operational owner must control or influence capacity, admission, priority, service discipline, demand shaping, or service commitments.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Avoided congestion collapse, credible service commitments, visible bottlenecks, and capacity decisions that account for variability and delay.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** System cannot absorb surge, failure, rework or recovery; or excess reserve imposes unjustified cost and hides problems.

**Historical origin.** Teletraffic loss/staffing, heavy traffic, safety stock and production-flow capacity-buffer traditions.

**How the property evolved.** Fixed safety factors evolved into service-level, many-server, robust/scenario, safety-stock and resilience-aware reserve policies.

**Important criticisms.** Parametric queue models can be powerful within domain but are brittle under heterogeneous, strategic, nonstationary, or feedback-coupled work; qualitative congestion properties transfer more widely than distributions.

**Known failure modes.** Average-rate fallacy, hidden queues, 100% utilisation targets, steady-state formulas in transient overload, false independence, and adding parallel workers behind a shared bottleneck.

**Lineage class.** HYBRID

**Mechanism.** Relate reserve to demand/service variability, tail/service consequence, recovery lead time, common-mode coupling and cost; review after regime change.

**Model assumptions.** Stationarity, independence, service discipline, routing, finite/infinite buffers, steady-state existence, and workload homogeneity are explicit where formulas rely on them.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use peak/backlog/recovery observations and a bounded reserve rule before fitting a full queue/inventory model.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Throughput, waiting, abandonment, service level, tail latency, cost, fairness, and overload consequences must not be collapsed into utilisation alone.

**Historical origin.** Priority queues, call-centre/service operations, scheduling dispatch rules and online resource allocation.

**How the property evolved.** Static priority queues broadened into fairness-aware, deadline-aware, state-dependent and learning-assisted policies.

**Important criticisms.** Parametric queue models can be powerful within domain but are brittle under heterogeneous, strategic, nonstationary, or feedback-coupled work; qualitative congestion properties transfer more widely than distributions.

**Known failure modes.** Average-rate fallacy, hidden queues, 100% utilisation targets, steady-state formulas in transient overload, false independence, and adding parallel workers behind a shared bottleneck.

**Lineage class.** HYBRID

**Evolved form in this source.** Service discipline is an owned policy with starvation, gaming, fairness and overload tests, not an accidental queue order.

**Mechanism.** Define admission, class, priority, preemption, ageing, fairness and overload rules; test class-specific waiting and incentive effects.

**Model assumptions.** Stationarity, independence, service discipline, routing, finite/infinite buffers, steady-state existence, and workload homogeneity are explicit where formulas rely on them.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use a transparent priority/ageing rule with explicit exceptions; avoid queueing optimisation when consequences clearly order work.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Throughput, waiting, abandonment, service level, tail latency, cost, fairness, and overload consequences must not be collapsed into utilisation alone.

**Open questions.** What priority/fairness rules remain legitimate when stakeholder utilities are incomparable?

**Original form.** Specify order of service, priorities, loss/admission and preemption rules.

**Primary sources.** S017; S056; S112

**Problem it addressed.** Overload decisions occur implicitly through first-come, escalation, visibility, politics or hidden abandonment.

**Property id.** P026

**Property name.** Make admission, priority, and service discipline explicit

**Relationship class.** QUEUEING_OR_INVENTORY_NATIVE

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Servers, people, processors, shared acceptance resources, buffers, and surge/reserve capacity are represented as coupled resources.

**Trigger or context.** Overload, heterogeneous consequences, deadlines, finite capacity, competing classes or safety/mission priorities.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Burstiness, nonstationarity, failures, retrials, rare surges, correlated demand, and model-form uncertainty are stress-tested when consequential.

**Requalification.** **Claim ids.** C026
