## P027 — Distinguish transient, nonstationary, overloaded, and feedback-congested regimes

**Ceremony vs property.** A steady-state queue model is not the default; regime classification is the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S055; S056

**Critical sources.** S056; S096; S097

**Status at this source freeze.** QUEUE_CAPACITY_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Arrival/demand timing, service-time or capacity behaviour, variability, retries, blocking, priority classes, and backlog/WIP measurements at decision-relevant granularity.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Average conditions conceal startup, surge, structural break, retry storm, overload persistence or feedback collapse.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** An operational owner must control or influence capacity, admission, priority, service discipline, demand shaping, or service commitments.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Average conditions conceal startup, surge, structural break, retry storm, overload persistence or feedback collapse.

**Historical origin.** Time-varying queues, many-server staffing, tail-at-scale systems and modern queueing/prediction research.

**How the property evolved.** Stationary queueing evolved into transient approximations, time-varying networks, autoscaling, admission control and feedback-aware models.

**Important criticisms.** Parametric queue models can be powerful within domain but are brittle under heterogeneous, strategic, nonstationary, or feedback-coupled work; qualitative congestion properties transfer more widely than distributions.

**Known failure modes.** Average-rate fallacy, hidden queues, 100% utilisation targets, steady-state formulas in transient overload, false independence, and adding parallel workers behind a shared bottleneck.

**Lineage class.** QUEUEING_AND_TELETRAFFIC_LINEAGE

**Evolved form in this source.** Capacity governance is regime-aware and monitors whether model assumptions and stability conditions currently hold.

**Mechanism.** Classify regime; analyse transient trajectories, time-varying staffing, overload duration, retry/feedback and recovery; fail closed when no stable steady state exists.

**Model assumptions.** Stationarity, independence, service discipline, routing, finite/infinite buffers, steady-state existence, and workload homogeneity are explicit where formulas rely on them.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Plot time series of demand, capacity, backlog and recovery; reject steady-state inference when backlog trends or regimes shift.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Throughput, waiting, abandonment, service level, tail latency, cost, fairness, and overload consequences must not be collapsed into utilisation alone.

**Open questions.** How should feedback from adaptive users and automated retries be identified in partially observed systems?

**Original form.** Move beyond stationary steady-state averages to time-varying arrival rates and transient performance.

**Primary sources.** S052; S053; S054; S055

**Problem it addressed.** Steady-state formulas are applied when demand, capacity or routing changes faster than the system equilibrates.

**Ceremony vs property.** A full queueing-network solution is optional; explicit coupling and shared bottlenecks are the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S055

**Critical sources.** S056; S057

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Arrival/demand timing, service-time or capacity behaviour, variability, retries, blocking, priority classes, and backlog/WIP measurements at decision-relevant granularity.

**Decision or consumer.** The owner of staffing, capacity, admission, prioritisation, or service-level policy.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Adding capacity or parallelism at one stage worsens congestion elsewhere or increases total completion time/cost.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** An operational owner must control or influence capacity, admission, priority, service discipline, demand shaping, or service commitments.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Historical origin.** Jackson queueing networks, network flow, production systems and large-scale computing/service systems.

**How the property evolved.** Product-form queue networks broadened into non-product-form, finite-capacity, fork-join, tail, simulation and feedback-control models.

**Mechanism.** Map routing, feedback/rework probabilities, shared resources, finite buffers and blocking; evaluate end-to-end throughput and tail consequence.

**Model assumptions.** Stationarity, independence, service discipline, routing, finite/infinite buffers, steady-state existence, and workload homogeneity are explicit where formulas rely on them.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Draw a flow/queue map and identify shared resources/retry loops before using network formulas or simulation.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Throughput, waiting, abandonment, service level, tail latency, cost, fairness, and overload consequences must not be collapsed into utilisation alone.

**Open questions.** What minimal network abstraction preserves fork-join and shared-acceptance delay in agentic systems?

**Original form.** Represent routing among several service stations and coupled waiting lines.

**Primary sources.** S009; S014; S054; S055

**Problem it addressed.** A single-stage model ignores downstream blocking, shared acceptance resources, rework loops or retry amplification.

## P029 — Schedule parallel work from real precedence, resource, and acceptance conflicts

**Ceremony vs property.** A Gantt or optimisation solver is optional; truthful dependency/resource boundaries are the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S063

**Critical sources.** S063; S065; S066

**Status at this source freeze.** SCHEDULING_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Precedence, release dates, resource/skill needs, setup and blocking conditions, duration uncertainty, deadlines, current progress, and shared validation capacity.

**Decision or consumer.** The operational coordinator and resource owners who can execute and revise the schedule.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Unnecessary delay from false dependencies, or collision/rework from unsafe parallelism.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** A schedule owner must be able to allocate constrained resources, resolve conflicts, change priorities, and re-plan after state changes.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S062

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** MODERATE

**Empirical comparative strength.** HIGH

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** HIGH

**Synthesis note.** Strong scheduling property with direct transfer to compute/service work.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Fewer resource conflicts, less avoidable blocking, earlier completion of genuinely independent work, and controlled adaptation to changed state.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Unnecessary delay from false dependencies, or collision/rework from unsafe parallelism.

**Historical origin.** Multiprocessor/list scheduling, critical-path methods, resource-constrained project scheduling and industrial multi-skill scheduling.

**How the property evolved.** Deterministic machine scheduling broadened into resource-constrained, multi-skill, multi-project and dynamic execution models.

**Important criticisms.** Optimal schedules are often fragile to duration and availability error; schedule stability and implementability can dominate nominal makespan.

**Known failure modes.** Confusing shared project labels with dependencies, deterministic critical-path worship, resource-infeasible parallelism, stale published plans, churn from over-reactive replanning, and schedule padding.

**Lineage class.** SCHEDULING_LINEAGE

**Evolved form in this source.** Parallelism is authorised by current conflict evidence and recomputed after state changes; neither issue-wide serialisation nor maximal concurrency is default.

**Mechanism.** Separate precedence from shared labels; map resource, skill, state, write/acceptance and external conflicts; schedule independent compatible work concurrently.

**Model assumptions.** Precedence and resource constraints are real; task durations, preemption, parallelism, calendars, and availability are not treated as certain unless justified.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Topological plus resource-conflict screening and a simple list/dispatch rule.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Completion time, tardiness, service, risk, stability, switching/setup cost, resource feasibility, and acceptance constraints are distinguished.

**Open questions.** How should acceptance and information-conflict resources be represented when they are not conventional machines?

**Original form.** Order activities subject to precedence and machine/resource constraints.

**Primary sources.** S017; S018; S019; S061; S062; S064

**Problem it addressed.** Work is serialised by administrative grouping or parallelised despite hidden resource/test/acceptance conflicts.

**Property id.** P029

**Property name.** Schedule parallel work from real precedence, resource, and acceptance conflicts

**Relationship class.** OPERATIONS_RESEARCH_NATIVE

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Machines, people, skills, processors, test/acceptance channels, external approvals, and switching costs are explicit.

**Trigger or context.** Multiple ready work units with potential dependency or resource interaction.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Breakdowns, rework, duration error, arrivals, resource loss, and changing priorities are represented through buffers, policies, scenarios, or rolling rescheduling as warranted.

## P030 — Treat resource ceilings as constraints, not utilisation targets

**Ceremony vs property.** A capacity dashboard is optional; preserving upper-bound semantics is the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S016

**Critical sources.** S016; S057

**Status at this source freeze.** SCHEDULING_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Precedence, release dates, resource/skill needs, setup and blocking conditions, duration uncertainty, deadlines, current progress, and shared validation capacity.

**Decision or consumer.** The operational coordinator and resource owners who can execute and revise the schedule.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Work is pulled prematurely, WIP/queues grow, option value is lost, or scarce acceptance capacity is saturated.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** A schedule owner must be able to allocate constrained resources, resolve conflicts, change priorities, and re-plan after state changes.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S062

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** MODERATE

**Contrary evidence strength.** MODERATE

**Empirical comparative strength.** MODERATE

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** LOW

**Synthesis note.** Strong formal and operational distinction.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Fewer resource conflicts, less avoidable blocking, earlier completion of genuinely independent work, and controlled adaptation to changed state.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Work is pulled prematurely, WIP/queues grow, option value is lost, or scarce acceptance capacity is saturated.

**Historical origin.** Mathematical programming capacity constraints, RCPSP and capacity-planning practice.

**How the property evolved.** Static resource constraints broadened toward rolling budgets, shared skills, two-time-scale provisioning and queue-aware scheduling.

**Important criticisms.** Optimal schedules are often fragile to duration and availability error; schedule stability and implementability can dominate nominal makespan.

**Known failure modes.** Confusing shared project labels with dependencies, deterministic critical-path worship, resource-infeasible parallelism, stale published plans, churn from over-reactive replanning, and schedule padding.

**Lineage class.** SCHEDULING_LINEAGE

**Evolved form in this source.** Ceilings constrain admissible actions while utilisation remains an observed trade-off with delay, reserve and switching cost.

**Mechanism.** Represent ceilings and budgets as upper bounds; optimise/dispatch against completion and consequence, allowing intentional idle time where marginal work is harmful or low value.

**Model assumptions.** Precedence and resource constraints are real; task durations, preemption, parallelism, calendars, and availability are not treated as certain unless justified.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Enforce the ceiling and choose the highest-value feasible ready work; do not add filler work to hit a utilisation metric.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Completion time, tardiness, service, risk, stability, switching/setup cost, resource feasibility, and acceptance constraints are distinguished.

**Open questions.** When should unused capacity trigger investigation versus be accepted as rational reserve?

**Original form.** Limit simultaneous activity by available renewable/nonrenewable resources.

**Primary sources.** S007; S010; S061; S062; S064; S112

**Problem it addressed.** A maximum permitted resource level is converted into an instruction to keep every resource maximally busy.

## P031 — Represent uncertain duration, release, setup, blocking, and deadline structure

**Ceremony vs property.** PERT beta assumptions and fixed calendars are not required; timing and blocking truthfulness is.

**Contrary evidence.** S065; S066

**Critical sources.** S065; S066

**Status at this source freeze.** SCHEDULING_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Precedence, release dates, resource/skill needs, setup and blocking conditions, duration uncertainty, deadlines, current progress, and shared validation capacity.

**Decision or consumer.** The operational coordinator and resource owners who can execute and revise the schedule.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Critical path, completion date and resource plan are falsely precise and collapse under ordinary variability.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** A schedule owner must be able to allocate constrained resources, resolve conflicts, change priorities, and re-plan after state changes.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S062; S063

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** HIGH

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** HIGH

**Synthesis note.** Strong structural property; probability forms are assumption-sensitive.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Fewer resource conflicts, less avoidable blocking, earlier completion of genuinely independent work, and controlled adaptation to changed state.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Critical path, completion date and resource plan are falsely precise and collapse under ordinary variability.

**Historical origin.** PERT, stochastic/dynamic scheduling, manufacturing execution and RCPSP extensions.

**How the property evolved.** Deterministic networks evolved into stochastic, robust, scenario, simulation and reactive scheduling with explicit execution feedback.

**Important criticisms.** Optimal schedules are often fragile to duration and availability error; schedule stability and implementability can dominate nominal makespan.

**Known failure modes.** Confusing shared project labels with dependencies, deterministic critical-path worship, resource-infeasible parallelism, stale published plans, churn from over-reactive replanning, and schedule padding.

**Lineage class.** SCHEDULING_LINEAGE

**Evolved form in this source.** Published times are conditional forecasts with uncertainty and conflict semantics, not commitments that reality must obey.

**Mechanism.** Classify timing uncertainty and structural constraints; use ranges/distributions only when supported; include setup, handoff, blocking, calendars and deadline consequence.

**Model assumptions.** Precedence and resource constraints are real; task durations, preemption, parallelism, calendars, and availability are not treated as certain unless justified.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use three-point/range estimates and obvious setup/blocking checks without claiming PERT distributional precision.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Completion time, tardiness, service, risk, stability, switching/setup cost, resource feasibility, and acceptance constraints are distinguished.

**Open questions.** Which duration distributions, if any, are defensible for novel knowledge work?

**Original form.** Project networks represented uncertain activity times; machine scheduling represented release dates, setup and due dates.

**Primary sources.** S018; S060; S063; S064; S065; S066

**Problem it addressed.** A schedule treats uncertain durations and resource availability as fixed, or omits setup/blocking and release constraints.

**Property id.** P031

**Property name.** Represent uncertain duration, release, setup, blocking, and deadline structure

**Relationship class.** OPERATIONS_RESEARCH_NATIVE

## P032 — Recompute schedules and allocations after material state change

**Ceremony vs property.** Daily replanning rituals are optional; material state-triggered currentness is the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S063

**Critical sources.** S066

**Status at this source freeze.** SCHEDULING_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Precedence, release dates, resource/skill needs, setup and blocking conditions, duration uncertainty, deadlines, current progress, and shared validation capacity.

**Decision or consumer.** The operational coordinator and resource owners who can execute and revise the schedule.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Resources follow a stale critical path or allocation while the actual bottleneck and feasible work have moved.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** A schedule owner must be able to allocate constrained resources, resolve conflicts, change priorities, and re-plan after state changes.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S062; S063

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** MODERATE

**Empirical comparative strength.** HIGH

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** HIGH

**Synthesis note.** Strong mature scheduling property.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Fewer resource conflicts, less avoidable blocking, earlier completion of genuinely independent work, and controlled adaptation to changed state.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Resources follow a stale critical path or allocation while the actual bottleneck and feasible work have moved.

**Historical origin.** Reactive/dynamic scheduling, rolling-horizon control, time-varying queue staffing and online optimisation.

**How the property evolved.** Baseline schedules evolved into reactive, predictive–reactive, rolling-horizon and online scheduling policies.

**Important criticisms.** Optimal schedules are often fragile to duration and availability error; schedule stability and implementability can dominate nominal makespan.

**Known failure modes.** Confusing shared project labels with dependencies, deterministic critical-path worship, resource-infeasible parallelism, stale published plans, churn from over-reactive replanning, and schedule padding.

**Lineage class.** HYBRID

**Evolved form in this source.** The schedule is a current decision policy with explicit recomputation and freeze/stability rules, not an archival chart.

**Mechanism.** Define event and threshold triggers; update current state; recompute feasible ready work and resource allocation; preserve commitments/switching costs where warranted.

**Model assumptions.** Precedence and resource constraints are real; task durations, preemption, parallelism, calendars, and availability are not treated as certain unless justified.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Re-run a simple priority/resource-conflict rule on current state instead of rebuilding a full optimisation.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Completion time, tardiness, service, risk, stability, switching/setup cost, resource feasibility, and acceptance constraints are distinguished.

**Open questions.** Which triggers minimise stale-plan loss without inducing excessive replanning churn?

**Original form.** Monitor execution and reschedule after disruptions or new arrivals.

**Primary sources.** S052; S053; S060; S063; S064; S112

**Problem it addressed.** A schedule is treated as a fixed prediction even after durations, resources, priorities or work state change.

## P033 — Balance schedule stability against adaptive replanning and switching costs

**Ceremony vs property.** A fixed freeze window is not universal; value-aware stability is the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S112

**Critical sources.** S060

**Status at this source freeze.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Precedence, release dates, resource/skill needs, setup and blocking conditions, duration uncertainty, deadlines, current progress, and shared validation capacity.

**Decision or consumer.** The operational coordinator and resource owners who can execute and revise the schedule.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Over-reactive schedules thrash people/resources; under-reactive schedules miss genuine value or risk changes.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** A schedule owner must be able to allocate constrained resources, resolve conflicts, change priorities, and re-plan after state changes.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S063

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** MODERATE

**Empirical comparative strength.** MODERATE

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** MODERATE

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** HIGH

**Synthesis note.** Strong tension resolution; parameters are highly contextual.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Fewer resource conflicts, less avoidable blocking, earlier completion of genuinely independent work, and controlled adaptation to changed state.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Over-reactive schedules thrash people/resources; under-reactive schedules miss genuine value or risk changes.

**Historical origin.** Predictive–reactive scheduling, match-up/stability scheduling, rolling horizons and online optimisation with switching costs.

**How the property evolved.** Baseline adherence evolved into stability metrics, match-up scheduling, rolling horizons and explicit switching-cost online models.

**Important criticisms.** Optimal schedules are often fragile to duration and availability error; schedule stability and implementability can dominate nominal makespan.

**Known failure modes.** Confusing shared project labels with dependencies, deterministic critical-path worship, resource-infeasible parallelism, stale published plans, churn from over-reactive replanning, and schedule padding.

**Lineage class.** SEQUENTIAL_OR_ONLINE_DECISION_LINEAGE

**Evolved form in this source.** Replan only when expected improvement or risk reduction exceeds disruption cost, with exceptions for hard constraints.

**Mechanism.** Include switching, commitment, communication and learning costs; use hysteresis/freeze windows or threshold triggers; compare improvement against disruption cost.

**Model assumptions.** Precedence and resource constraints are real; task durations, preemption, parallelism, calendars, and availability are not treated as certain unless justified.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use a minimum-improvement threshold and protect near-term committed work unless safety or feasibility changes.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Completion time, tardiness, service, risk, stability, switching/setup cost, resource feasibility, and acceptance constraints are distinguished.

**Open questions.** How should cognitive and trust costs of schedule changes be measured?

**Original form.** Reschedule after disruption while penalising deviation from the baseline or resource switching.

**Primary sources.** S063; S064; S066; S112

**Problem it addressed.** Either the plan remains stale or every small change causes churn, loss of trust and switching overhead.

**Property id.** P033

**Property name.** Balance schedule stability against adaptive replanning and switching costs

**Relationship class.** HYBRID_RESOLUTION

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Machines, people, skills, processors, test/acceptance channels, external approvals, and switching costs are explicit.

## P034 — Use work-conserving execution only within conflict, option, safety, and congestion bounds

**Ceremony vs property.** A blanket non-idling rule is rejected; bounded productive dispatch is retained.

**Contrary evidence.** S035; S112

**Critical sources.** S016; S035

**Status at this source freeze.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Precedence, release dates, resource/skill needs, setup and blocking conditions, duration uncertainty, deadlines, current progress, and shared validation capacity.

**Decision or consumer.** The operational coordinator and resource owners who can execute and revise the schedule.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Starting work consumes scarce WIP/acceptance capacity, blocks higher-value arrivals, creates setup costs, destroys options or increases risk.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** A schedule owner must be able to allocate constrained resources, resolve conflicts, change priorities, and re-plan after state changes.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S054

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** MODERATE

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** HIGH

**Synthesis note.** Retained only in bounded form; blanket utilisation translation rejected.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Fewer resource conflicts, less avoidable blocking, earlier completion of genuinely independent work, and controlled adaptation to changed state.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Starting work consumes scarce WIP/acceptance capacity, blocks higher-value arrivals, creates setup costs, destroys options or increases risk.

**Historical origin.** Scheduling theory, queueing service disciplines, online algorithms and capacity/option-value reasoning.

**How the property evolved.** Classical non-idling rules were narrowed by release/priority/setup/robustness/energy/switching and option-preservation contexts.

**Important criticisms.** Optimal schedules are often fragile to duration and availability error; schedule stability and implementability can dominate nominal makespan.

**Known failure modes.** Confusing shared project labels with dependencies, deterministic critical-path worship, resource-infeasible parallelism, stale published plans, churn from over-reactive replanning, and schedule padding.

**Lineage class.** HYBRID

**Evolved form in this source.** Execution is work-conserving relative to a truthful eligibility and consequence model, not relative to raw worker/machine availability.

**Mechanism.** Define eligible work and conflict bounds; compare marginal start value with queue, setup, option, safety and switching consequences; allow intentional idle time when justified.

**Model assumptions.** Precedence and resource constraints are real; task durations, preemption, parallelism, calendars, and availability are not treated as certain unless justified.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Start the highest-value conflict-free work; remain idle when no eligible work has positive net consequence.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Completion time, tardiness, service, risk, stability, switching/setup cost, resource feasibility, and acceptance constraints are distinguished.

**Open questions.** What minimal eligibility model prevents premature work without recreating a heavy scheduler?

**Original form.** Do not idle a machine when eligible work is waiting, under objectives where non-idling is optimal or admissible.

**Primary sources.** S017; S054; S057; S112

**Problem it addressed.** ‘Always keep resources busy’ is generalised beyond the assumptions that justify non-idling.

**Property id.** P034

**Property name.** Use work-conserving execution only within conflict, option, safety, and congestion bounds

**Relationship class.** HYBRID_RESOLUTION

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Machines, people, skills, processors, test/acceptance channels, external approvals, and switching costs are explicit.

**Trigger or context.** Ready work exists but starting it could create shared-resource conflict, WIP, lock-in, setup, safety or priority harm.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Breakdowns, rework, duration error, arrivals, resource loss, and changing priorities are represented through buffers, policies, scenarios, or rolling rescheduling as warranted.

## P035 — Value inventory, buffers, slack, and reserve conditionally rather than categorically

**Ceremony vs property.** EOQ, safety-stock formula or WIP board is optional; conditional buffer economics is the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S036; S058

**Critical sources.** S036; S057

**Status at this source freeze.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Demand/arrival variability, lead times, replenishment/recovery time, perishability, shortage consequence, coupling, and cost of reserve.

**Decision or consumer.** The owner of capacity, inventory, commitment timing, or recovery policy.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Too little buffer causes shortages, coupling and recovery failure; too much creates cost, obsolescence, delay and hidden WIP.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** The owner must control inventory, time reserve, WIP limits, capacity reserve, or staged commitment and be accountable for both shortage and holding costs.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S058; S059

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** MODERATE

**Empirical comparative strength.** HIGH

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** HIGH

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** HIGH

**Synthesis note.** Strong conditional property; numeric models are domain-bound.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Lower shortage and overload exposure, better decoupling, and preserved recovery or commitment options at an explicit cost.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Too little buffer causes shortages, coupling and recovery failure; too much creates cost, obsolescence, delay and hidden WIP.

**Historical origin.** EOQ, stochastic inventory, safety stock, production-flow and real-option traditions.

**How the property evolved.** Deterministic lot size evolved into stochastic service-level, multi-echelon, robust, perishable and option-aware buffer decisions.

**Important criticisms.** Buffer value is context-specific; excessive reserve can increase cost, obsolescence, delay, moral hazard, and hidden WIP.

**Known failure modes.** Universal zero-inventory or universal-safety-stock rules, buffers that hide defects, stale reserve levels, and reserve converted into a utilisation target.

**Lineage class.** INVENTORY_AND_CAPACITY_LINEAGE

**Evolved form in this source.** Every buffer has an owned purpose, cost, trigger, sizing basis and retirement/recalibration rule.

**Mechanism.** Classify buffer purpose; model variability, lead/recovery time, service/shortage consequence, coupling, perishability and holding cost; place decoupling where it changes flow.

**Model assumptions.** Demand, replenishment, substitution, service-level, and cost structures fit the operating context sufficiently for quantitative sizing.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use observed stockouts/backlogs, lead time and holding/shortage asymmetry to set a bounded rule; avoid formula transfer without fit.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Service, waiting, stockout, obsolescence, carrying cost, recovery, coupling, and option value are represented rather than treating all slack as waste or all buffer as safety.

**Open questions.** How should information and attention buffers be valued in non-repeatable work?

**Original form.** Balance setup/order, holding, shortage and service costs; retain stock against uncertain demand.

**Primary sources.** S003; S004; S034; S035; S058; S059

**Problem it addressed.** ‘Inventory is waste’ or ‘slack is safety’ is treated as a universal rule.

**Property id.** P035

**Property name.** Value inventory, buffers, slack, and reserve conditionally rather than categorically

**Relationship class.** QUEUEING_OR_INVENTORY_NATIVE

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Inventory, spare capacity, time, cash, attention, and recovery resources are treated as costly options.

**Trigger or context.** Variable demand or supply, long recovery/replenishment, coupled stages, service commitments, irreversible shortages or failure recovery.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Demand, supply, duration, failure, and recovery uncertainty determine whether reserve has value.

**Requalification.** **Claim ids.** C035

**Ceremony vs property.** A newsvendor formula is optional; owned overage–underage reasoning is the property.

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Demand/arrival variability, lead times, replenishment/recovery time, perishability, shortage consequence, coupling, and cost of reserve.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Capacity/inventory is over- or under-provisioned relative to real shortage, delay and holding consequences.

## P037 — Use mathematical optimisation only when decision complexity and value warrant it

**Ceremony vs property.** No solver, language or formulation is mandatory; resource-discriminating optimisation is the property only when warranted.

**Contrary evidence.** S098

**Critical sources.** S048; S070; S098; S101

**Status at this source freeze.** OPTIMISATION_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Coefficients, feasible sets, capacities, costs, forecasts, and current state have provenance and uncertainty appropriate to their leverage on the solution.

**Decision or consumer.** The owner of the allocation, design, policy, or schedule and the operators who must implement it.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Either needless model/solver burden or avoidable resource loss from crude manual allocation.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** The decision owner must approve the represented objective, constraints, alternatives, tolerances, and implementation rules; solver output is advice unless authority is explicitly automated.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S062; S101

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** HIGH

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** LOW

**Synthesis note.** Optimisation retained conditionally, not universally.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Transparent resource trade-offs and high-value improvements in complex feasible spaces where simpler discrimination is inadequate.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Either needless model/solver burden or avoidable resource loss from crude manual allocation.

**Historical origin.** Linear/integer programming, assignment, network flow, scheduling and later computational optimisation.

**How the property evolved.** Exact LP broadened to mixed-integer, nonlinear, decomposition, approximation, heuristics and rolling optimisation, while mature practice re-emphasises formulation and implementation.

**Important criticisms.** Formal optimality is conditional on the model; small optimality gaps can coexist with large real-world regret, and more exact algorithms do not repair misspecification.

**Known failure modes.** Optimising the wrong objective, infeasible or unenforceable constraints, numerical false precision, brittle point estimates, local optimum/global harm, and solver optimality worship.

**Lineage class.** MATHEMATICAL_PROGRAMMING_LINEAGE

**Evolved form in this source.** Optimisation is a proportionate decision instrument with baseline comparison, validity evidence, sensitivity and implementability—not a badge.

**Mechanism.** Estimate decision stakes, feasible-space complexity, coupling, repeatability and achievable improvement; compare simple baselines and escalate to exact/approximate optimisation only when net value is positive.

**Model assumptions.** Functional form, linearity/convexity, integrality, independence, horizon, decomposition, and numerical tolerances are stated and tested where material.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Direct feasibility/dominance test, greedy/list heuristic or small enumeration with explicit regret bound where possible.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The mathematical objective is traceable to valued consequences; hard constraints are enforceable; penalties, scaling, and proxies are challengeable.

**Open questions.** How should expected implementation benefit be estimated before building the model?

**Original form.** Search a large constrained feasible set for a best objective value.

**Primary sources.** S007; S008; S009; S010; S017; S073

**Problem it addressed.** Optimisation is applied ceremonially to simple, dominated or already-discriminated decisions, or omitted where combinatorial/resource complexity has high consequence.

**Relationship class.** MATHEMATICAL_PROGRAMMING_NATIVE

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Sensitivity, out-of-sample performance, scenario or ambiguity analysis, and model-form uncertainty are addressed according to consequence.

**Ceremony vs property.** Solver diagnostics help but semantic constraint ownership is the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S098

**Critical sources.** S070; S098; S101

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Coefficients, feasible sets, capacities, costs, forecasts, and current state have provenance and uncertainty appropriate to their leverage on the solution.

**Decision or consumer.** The owner of the allocation, design, policy, or schedule and the operators who must implement it.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Decision makers abandon feasible real options, relax a real hard constraint, or deploy a solution impossible under omitted local conditions.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** The decision owner must approve the represented objective, constraints, alternatives, tolerances, and implementation rules; solver output is advice unless authority is explicitly automated.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Mechanism.** Trace infeasibility to conflicting assumptions/data/constraints; use slack and conflict diagnostics with semantic ownership; field-check feasibility and execution sequence.

**Model assumptions.** Functional form, linearity/convexity, integrality, independence, horizon, decomposition, and numerical tolerances are stated and tested where material.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The mathematical objective is traceable to valued consequences; hard constraints are enforceable; penalties, scaling, and proxies are challengeable.

**Original form.** Construct objective and feasible set; detect infeasible or unbounded models.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Inspect binding constraints and perform a small finite change scenario rather than treating an infinitesimal dual as truth.

**Open questions.** When do discrete or strategic responses invalidate marginal capacity values?

**Original form.** Dual variables express marginal objective value of relaxing represented constraints under local/model conditions.

## P041 — Distinguish variability, parameter uncertainty, ambiguity, model-form error, and nonstationarity

**Ceremony vs property.** A probability distribution is optional and sometimes misleading; uncertainty typing is the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S044; S088; S089

**Critical sources.** S088; S089

**Status at this source freeze.** UNCERTAINTY_RECOURSE_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Probability estimates, scenarios, intervals, ambiguity sets, correlations, tail evidence, and recourse costs have provenance and calibration limits.

**Decision or consumer.** The owner who bears downside and can fund hedges, reserves, recourse, or staged commitment.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** A method robust to sampled variability is claimed robust to misspecified models, structural breaks or unknown state spaces.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** The decision owner must define acceptable downside, risk, confidence, robustness, recourse, and the cost of conservatism.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S087

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** HIGH

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** MODERATE

**Replication or multi study strength.** HIGH

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** HIGH

**Synthesis note.** Strong mature uncertainty boundary.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Decisions that remain acceptable across credible states and can adapt or recover when the realised state differs from the nominal forecast.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** A method robust to sampled variability is claimed robust to misspecified models, structural breaks or unknown state spaces.

**Historical origin.** Bayesian/Savage decision theory, Ellsberg ambiguity, stochastic/robust optimisation and simulation uncertainty analysis.

**How the property evolved.** Risk-under-known-probabilities expanded toward ambiguity, distributional uncertainty, model discrepancy, drift and adaptive learning.

**Important criticisms.** Robust, stochastic, chance-constrained, and distributionally robust methods protect against different uncertainty statements; none is universally mature without fit and calibration evidence.

**Known failure modes.** Invented distributions, narrow scenarios, blanket worst-case conservatism, ambiguity sets tuned after the fact, infeasible recourse, and robustness to parameters but not model class.

**Lineage class.** HYBRID

**Evolved form in this source.** The uncertainty claim states what is known, estimated, bounded, disputed or unmodelled, and does not overgeneralise protection.

**Mechanism.** Classify uncertainty source and epistemic status; select sensitivity, stochastic, robust, distributionally robust, scenario, adaptive or abstention mechanisms accordingly.

**Model assumptions.** Known probabilities, uncertainty-set coverage, distributional family, stage structure, observability, recourse feasibility, and stationarity are explicit.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use separate labels and bounds; avoid assigning probabilities where identification is weak.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Expected performance, tail consequence, regret, reliability, service, recourse cost, and opportunity cost are distinguished.

**Open questions.** How can deep/model uncertainty be operationalised without arbitrary ambiguity sets?

**Original form.** Represent known probability distributions or subjective probabilities for uncertain states.

**Primary sources.** S042; S044; S027; S028; S029; S086; S087

**Problem it addressed.** All uncertainty is collapsed into one distribution, confidence interval or scenario list.

**Property id.** P041

**Property name.** Distinguish variability, parameter uncertainty, ambiguity, model-form error, and nonstationarity

**Relationship class.** STATISTICAL_IMPORT_OR_SHARED_ANCESTRY

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Reserve, hedging, recourse capacity, sensing, reconfiguration, and opportunity cost are represented.

**Trigger or context.** Material uncertain demand, duration, cost, state transition, model class, preference or external regime.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Aleatory variability, parameter uncertainty, model-form uncertainty, ambiguity, nonstationarity, and unknown unknowns are not conflated.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** A lower-error forecast yields higher shortage, congestion, inventory, service or intervention cost; optimisation treats an estimate as known truth.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** The decision owner must define acceptable downside, risk, confidence, robustness, recourse, and the cost of conservatism.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Historical origin.** Probabilistic forecasting, proper scoring rules, stochastic inventory/capacity models and forecast-combination research.

**Mechanism.** Use calibrated probabilistic forecasts or explicit error scenarios; propagate uncertainty into the decision; evaluate by out-of-sample decision consequence as well as predictive score; test bias, structural break and feedback.

**Model assumptions.** Known probabilities, uncertainty-set coverage, distributional family, stage structure, observability, recourse feasibility, and stationarity are explicit.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use a conservative interval, break-even error bound, or simple high/low demand discriminator when a full predictive distribution cannot alter the chosen action.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Expected performance, tail consequence, regret, reliability, service, recourse cost, and opportunity cost are distinguished.

**Open questions.** Which predictive improvements retain value after optimisation, operator adjustment and regime change?

**Original form.** Estimate demand or state, then insert a point forecast or distribution into an operational model.

## P047 — Use state-contingent policies and rolling re-optimisation for revisable decisions

**Ceremony vs property.** Continuous re-solving is not required; state-linked reconsideration and bounded commitment are the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S117; S119

**Critical sources.** S117; S103

**Status at this source freeze.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Data or measurement preconditions.** State observations, transition/feedback data, delay, censoring, intervention effects, and nonstationarity are measured or bounded.

**Decision or consumer.** The operator or automated controller with explicit authority and guardrails.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Stale allocations and schedules accumulate infeasibility, delay or cost while nominal adherence is mistaken for control.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** The policy owner must control observations, update cadence, action authority, exploration limits, and switching or lock-in costs.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S063; S112

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** MODERATE

**Empirical comparative strength.** MODERATE

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** HIGH

**Synthesis note.** Strong sequential principle; exact policy methods are state- and model-dependent.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** State-contingent action, controlled learning, and timely re-optimisation without pretending that a static plan predicts the future.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Stale allocations and schedules accumulate infeasibility, delay or cost while nominal adherence is mistaken for control.

**Historical origin.** Dynamic programming, Markov decision processes, receding/rolling-horizon planning, online optimisation and dynamic scheduling.

**How the property evolved.** Bellman recursion and MDPs broadened into rolling-horizon scheduling, model-predictive and online optimisation, learning-augmented policies and human override.

**Important criticisms.** Formal regret or policy guarantees are environment- and model-dependent; reinforcement learning is one translation, not the universal endpoint.

**Known failure modes.** One-shot plans in changing systems, oscillatory replanning, reward misspecification, unsafe exploration, delayed-feedback bias, and lock-in ignored by rolling optimisation.

**Lineage class.** SEQUENTIAL_OR_ONLINE_DECISION_LINEAGE

**Evolved form in this source.** The operational object is a governed update policy with state/currentness evidence—not a frozen schedule pretending to predict the future.

**Mechanism.** Define observable state, review/replan events, horizon, frozen commitments, switching costs, recourse and authority; re-estimate and re-optimise only when changed state can alter action materially.

**Model assumptions.** State sufficiency, transition structure, feedback timing, horizon/discounting, exploration safety, and policy class are explicit.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use simple event-triggered rules or a short rolling priority list when exact dynamic optimisation is unnecessary.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Immediate reward/cost, future state, learning value, safety/constraint violation, switching cost, and long-horizon consequences are represented.

**Open questions.** What event threshold best balances responsiveness against replanning churn and implementation instability?

**Original form.** Choose a policy mapping observed state to action rather than a single open-loop action sequence.

**Problem it addressed.** A published one-shot plan remains authoritative after demand, capacity, task completion, failures or information have changed.

## P048 — Trade learning and exploration against operating consequence and information value

**Ceremony vs property.** An RL agent is not required; bounded learning action with positive decision value is the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S117; S105; S110

**Critical sources.** S117; S105; S110

**Status at this source freeze.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Data or measurement preconditions.** State observations, transition/feedback data, delay, censoring, intervention effects, and nonstationarity are measured or bounded.

**Decision or consumer.** The operator or automated controller with explicit authority and guardrails.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** The system either never learns a superior policy or treats live operations as a cost-free experiment without authority, safeguards or stopping rules.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** The policy owner must control observations, update cadence, action authority, exploration limits, and switching or lock-in costs.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S104; S117

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** MODERATE

**Field or operational strength.** MODERATE

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** MODERATE

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** HIGH

**Synthesis note.** Formally strong; transferable field evidence is bounded by safety, feedback and stationarity assumptions.

**Transferability strength.** MODERATE

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** State-contingent action, controlled learning, and timely re-optimisation without pretending that a static plan predicts the future.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** The system either never learns a superior policy or treats live operations as a cost-free experiment without authority, safeguards or stopping rules.

**Historical origin.** Bayesian experimentation, bandits, sequential decision theory, reinforcement learning and value-of-information/value-of-computation research.

**How the property evolved.** Bayesian sampling and bandits expanded into constrained/safe RL, adaptive experimentation, value-of-computation and learning-augmented algorithms.

**Important criticisms.** Formal regret or policy guarantees are environment- and model-dependent; reinforcement learning is one translation, not the universal endpoint.

**Known failure modes.** One-shot plans in changing systems, oscillatory replanning, reward misspecification, unsafe exploration, delayed-feedback bias, and lock-in ignored by rolling optimisation.

**Lineage class.** SEQUENTIAL_OR_ONLINE_DECISION_LINEAGE

**Evolved form in this source.** Exploration is an authorised information investment with explicit consequence, transfer horizon, guardrails and stop/reopen rule—not an assumed virtue.

**Mechanism.** Represent information gain, regret/opportunity cost, safety and hard constraints, horizon, reversibility and learning transfer; use bounded experiments, pilots or safe exploration only when expected future decision value exceeds cost.

**Model assumptions.** State sufficiency, transition structure, feedback timing, horizon/discounting, exploration safety, and policy class are explicit.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use historical/offline evidence, a deterministic discriminator, or a small controlled pilot when full adaptive exploration is unnecessary or unsafe.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Immediate reward/cost, future state, learning value, safety/constraint violation, switching cost, and long-horizon consequences are represented.

**Open questions.** How should exploration be valued when data are endogenous and harmful outcomes are irreversible?

**Original form.** Choose actions that both produce reward and reveal information useful for later decisions.

**Primary sources.** S039; S049; S118; S119

**Problem it addressed.** Pure exploitation locks into a poorly known option, while indiscriminate exploration imposes avoidable cost or unsafe exposure.

**Property id.** P048

**Property name.** Trade learning and exploration against operating consequence and information value

**Relationship class.** HYBRID_RESOLUTION

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Sensing, computation, experimentation capacity, reconfiguration effort, and operational slack constrain adaptation.

**Trigger or context.** Repeated decisions with material uncertainty, learnable response, future reuse and safe/reversible experimentation.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Unknown dynamics, delayed feedback, drift, partial observability, and exploration risk are treated as first-class burdens.

## P049 — Represent delayed and partial feedback, switching cost, lock-in, and hysteresis

**Ceremony vs property.** Real-time dashboards and constant optimisation are optional; feedback-latency and change-cost discipline are the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S117

**Critical sources.** S117

**Data or measurement preconditions.** State observations, transition/feedback data, delay, censoring, intervention effects, and nonstationarity are measured or bounded.

**Decision or consumer.** The operator or automated controller with explicit authority and guardrails.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** The system chases stale signals, thrashes between near-equivalent actions, ignores commitment, or updates from selectively observed outcomes.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** The policy owner must control observations, update cadence, action authority, exploration limits, and switching or lock-in costs.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S096; S097

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** The system chases stale signals, thrashes between near-equivalent actions, ignores commitment, or updates from selectively observed outcomes.

**Historical origin.** Dynamic programming, control/online optimisation, setup/switching-cost scheduling and performative-feedback research.

**How the property evolved.** State-dependent switching costs and setup models expanded into online constraints, performative feedback and governance against automated oscillation.

**Important criticisms.** Formal regret or policy guarantees are environment- and model-dependent; reinforcement learning is one translation, not the universal endpoint.

**Known failure modes.** One-shot plans in changing systems, oscillatory replanning, reward misspecification, unsafe exploration, delayed-feedback bias, and lock-in ignored by rolling optimisation.

**Lineage class.** HYBRID

**Evolved form in this source.** Adaptation is responsive but not reflexive: updates account for what is observable, when it becomes informative, and what changing course costs.

**Mechanism.** Model observation delay and censoring, switching/setup cost, minimum hold times, lock-in, hysteresis bands and delayed-effect evaluation; distinguish absence of evidence from evidence of failure.

**Model assumptions.** State sufficiency, transition structure, feedback timing, horizon/discounting, exploration safety, and policy class are explicit.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use a cooldown/minimum-hold rule and explicit delayed review window before building a full state-space model.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Immediate reward/cost, future state, learning value, safety/constraint violation, switching cost, and long-horizon consequences are represented.

**Open questions.** How can delayed causal effects be separated from ordinary operational noise under adaptive policy?

**Original form.** State transitions and action costs depend on prior action; feedback may arrive after delay.

## P050 — Verify implementation and validate simulation against its decision purpose

**Ceremony vs property.** An elaborate V&V dossier is proportional to consequence; challengeable credibility evidence is the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S088; S089

**Critical sources.** S088; S089

**Status at this source freeze.** SIMULATION_OR_MODEL_VALIDITY_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Input distributions, correlations, initial conditions, warm-up, random streams, calibration data, and output measures are controlled and documented.

**Decision or consumer.** The decision owner and domain experts who can challenge model structure and interpret output.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** A simulation faithfully executes the wrong logic, fits history for the wrong reasons, or is reused outside its validated domain.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** A decision owner must specify the decision question, acceptance criteria, and how simulation evidence will alter action.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S084; S090

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** HIGH

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** MODERATE

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** HIGH

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** HIGH

**Synthesis note.** One of the strongest simulation properties; absolute validation is rejected.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Safer experimentation with complex stochastic systems and quantified consequence ranges when analytic or live experimentation is impractical.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** A simulation faithfully executes the wrong logic, fits history for the wrong reasons, or is reused outside its validated domain.

**Historical origin.** Discrete-event/Monte Carlo simulation methodology, model verification and validation, and engineering model-governance practice.

**How the property evolved.** Verification/validation checklists matured toward iterative credibility assessment, input/model-form uncertainty and decision-context governance rather than universal ‘validation’ claims.

**Important criticisms.** Verification can show correct implementation of the conceptual model, not truth of the conceptual model; validation is purpose- and domain-relative.

**Known failure modes.** Many replications of a wrong model, animation-as-validation, dependence between replications, unhandled transients, calibration to history mistaken for intervention validity, and simulation optimum worship.

**Lineage class.** SIMULATION_LINEAGE

**Evolved form in this source.** A simulation has a bounded credibility claim tied to a decision, state, data regime and consequence—not a certificate that the model is true.

**Mechanism.** Separate conceptual-model validity, code verification, data/input validity, operational/face comparison, sensitivity, predictive/decision validation and documented use domain; involve independent challenge where consequence warrants.

**Model assumptions.** Conceptual model, boundary, causal structure, stochastic inputs, stationarity, and extrapolation regime are explicit.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** For low-consequence questions, use hand checks, extreme cases and comparison with observed/simple analytical behaviour.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The model estimates decision-relevant consequences and uncertainty rather than maximising detail, animation realism, or run count.

**Open questions.** What external validation is feasible for one-off systems or interventions with no close historical analogue?

**Original form.** Check that the simulation is correctly implemented and adequately represents the real system for its intended use.

**Primary sources.** S084; S088; S089; S090

**Problem it addressed.** Detailed animation, calibration or plausible output is mistaken for decision-valid representation.

**Property id.** P050

**Property name.** Verify implementation and validate simulation against its decision purpose

**Relationship class.** OPERATIONS_RESEARCH_NATIVE

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Run budget, model-building effort, validation evidence, data acquisition, and analyst attention are finite.

**Trigger or context.** Simulation output materially changes design, capacity, schedule, policy, safety margin, investment or resource allocation.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Monte Carlo error, input uncertainty, parameter uncertainty, model-form uncertainty, rare-event coverage, and scenario uncertainty are separated.

**Requalification.** **Claim ids.** C050

## P051 — Control replications, transients, dependence, and input uncertainty in simulation evidence

**Ceremony vs property.** Thousands of runs are not inherently credible; error-controlled evidence at decision-relevant precision is.

**Contrary evidence.** S088; S089

**Critical sources.** S088; S089

**Status at this source freeze.** SIMULATION_OR_MODEL_VALIDITY_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Input distributions, correlations, initial conditions, warm-up, random streams, calibration data, and output measures are controlled and documented.

**Decision or consumer.** The decision owner and domain experts who can challenge model structure and interpret output.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Narrow confidence intervals around the wrong or biased model create false precision, especially for rare events and nonstationary systems.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** A decision owner must specify the decision question, acceptance criteria, and how simulation evidence will alter action.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S087; S111

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** HIGH

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** HIGH

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** HIGH

**Synthesis note.** Mature statistical property; it cannot eliminate structural model error.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Safer experimentation with complex stochastic systems and quantified consequence ranges when analytic or live experimentation is impractical.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Narrow confidence intervals around the wrong or biased model create false precision, especially for rare events and nonstationary systems.

**Historical origin.** Monte Carlo output analysis, regenerative/steady-state simulation, input modelling and variance/uncertainty analysis.

**How the property evolved.** Run-count rules evolved toward designed experiments, sequential stopping, input-uncertainty intervals, metamodels and integrated simulation optimisation.

**Important criticisms.** Verification can show correct implementation of the conceptual model, not truth of the conceptual model; validation is purpose- and domain-relative.

**Known failure modes.** Many replications of a wrong model, animation-as-validation, dependence between replications, unhandled transients, calibration to history mistaken for intervention validity, and simulation optimum worship.

**Lineage class.** SIMULATION_LINEAGE

**Evolved form in this source.** Replication budget is tied to decision precision; independent statistical error, input uncertainty and structural uncertainty are not conflated.

**Mechanism.** Define estimand and horizon; control random streams; diagnose warm-up/transients/autocorrelation; quantify input uncertainty; use variance reduction/rare-event methods when justified; report model-form uncertainty separately.

**Model assumptions.** Conceptual model, boundary, causal structure, stochastic inputs, stationarity, and extrapolation regime are explicit.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use common random numbers plus a few independent replications and a consequence-based precision target; stop when ranking is stable enough for the decision.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The model estimates decision-relevant consequences and uncertainty rather than maximising detail, animation realism, or run count.

**Open questions.** How should simulation budgets be allocated between replication, input learning and alternative model forms?

**Original form.** Use independent random replications, appropriate warm-up/run length and statistical output analysis.

**Primary sources.** S085; S086; S087; S090; S111

**Problem it addressed.** More runs reduce Monte Carlo noise but leave initialisation bias, dependence, input estimation and model-form uncertainty untouched.

**Property id.** P051

**Property name.** Control replications, transients, dependence, and input uncertainty in simulation evidence

**Relationship class.** STATISTICAL_IMPORT_OR_SHARED_ANCESTRY

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Run budget, model-building effort, validation evidence, data acquisition, and analyst attention are finite.

**Trigger or context.** Stochastic simulation comparisons whose differences are near decision thresholds or whose tail/steady-state claims matter.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Monte Carlo error, input uncertainty, parameter uncertainty, model-form uncertainty, rare-event coverage, and scenario uncertainty are separated.

**Requalification.** **Claim ids.** C051

## P052 — Test multi-criteria rankings against weights, scales, normalisation, thresholds, and method choice

**Ceremony vs property.** A scored matrix is optional; explicit, challengeable value trade-offs and sensitivity are the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S078; S079; S080

**Critical sources.** S078; S079; S080

**Status at this source freeze.** MULTI_CRITERIA_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Alternative performance evidence, scale definitions, normalisation rules, weights or preference statements, and uncertainty ranges are traceable.

**Decision or consumer.** The accountable owner and stakeholders whose values are represented or whose disagreement is preserved.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Small, politically convenient or post-hoc changes to weights, bounds, criteria or alternatives reverse the winner while the matrix hides that fragility.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** A legitimate decision owner and affected stakeholders must own criteria, thresholds, weights, vetoes, and aggregation rules.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S083

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** MODERATE

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** LOW

**Synthesis note.** Strong anti-false-objectivity property; no universally superior MCDA method.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Transparent, challengeable trade-offs and preservation of Pareto, veto, and stakeholder information that a single proxy would erase.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Small, politically convenient or post-hoc changes to weights, bounds, criteria or alternatives reverse the winner while the matrix hides that fragility.

**Historical origin.** MAUT/AHP/outranking and multi-objective decision analysis; rank-reversal and robustness criticism.

**How the property evolved.** Fixed weighted sums broadened toward MAUT, outranking, Pareto methods, stochastic/robust MCDA and sensitivity/acceptability analysis.

**Important criticisms.** Different MCDA methods embody different value models and can rank the same alternatives differently; explicit judgement is not eliminated by arithmetic.

**Known failure modes.** Rank reversal, arbitrary normalisation, false cardinal arithmetic on ordinal scores, compensating across hard limits, post-hoc weights, and spreadsheet objectivity theatre.

**Lineage class.** MULTI_CRITERIA_DECISION_LINEAGE

**Evolved form in this source.** The output is a conditional preference map and robustness statement, not a discovered objective ranking.

**Mechanism.** Validate scale type and independence assumptions; expose normalisation; run weight/score/method sensitivity, rank acceptability and dominance/non-compensatory checks; preserve ties or undecided sets where warranted.

**Model assumptions.** Preferential/utility independence, cardinality, compensability, commensurability, and aggregation-method assumptions are explicit.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use dominance, threshold and break-even-weight analysis; omit scoring when a hard constraint or clear dominance settles the choice.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Criteria meanings, scale type, non-compensatory limits, stakeholder conflicts, and uncertainty are explicit; a total score is not presumed to be value truth.

**Open questions.** When stakeholder utilities cannot be legitimately aggregated, which partial-order or deliberative output should control action?

**Original form.** Aggregate criterion performance using elicited weights, utility/value functions or pairwise comparison.

**Primary sources.** S041; S077; S078; S079; S080; S081; S082; S083

**Problem it addressed.** A numerical rank is presented as objective despite unstable preferences, incompatible scales, alternative-dependent normalisation or compensatory assumptions.

**Property id.** P052

**Property name.** Test multi-criteria rankings against weights, scales, normalisation, thresholds, and method choice

**Relationship class.** DECISION_ANALYSIS_NATIVE

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Elicitation, facilitation, dispute resolution, data collection, and sensitivity analysis costs are proportionate to consequence.

**Trigger or context.** Trade studies or MCDA where criteria conflict and the top alternatives are close or preference inputs are contested.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Weight, score, threshold, normalisation, and alternative-set sensitivity are analysed where they could change ranking or admissibility.

**Requalification.** **Claim ids.** C052
