## P053 — Detect and resist objective, constraint, data, queue, and reporting gaming

**Ceremony vs property.** More dashboards are not anti-gaming; independent consequence evidence and change control are.

**Contrary evidence.** S105; S106; S107; S108; S110

**Critical sources.** S105; S110

**Status at this source freeze.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Metric provenance, denominators, missingness, threshold behaviour, classification changes, and outcome measures independent of the target are monitored.

**Decision or consumer.** The consequence owner, affected stakeholders, and governance authority—not only the metric-producing unit.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** The optimisation surface improves while real consequence worsens; post-hoc model revision makes a preferred alternative appear optimal.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** The owner of the real consequence must be distinct from owners rewarded on the proxy, with authority to audit and revise measures.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S105; S096; S097

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** HIGH

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** MODERATE

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** HIGH

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** LOW

**Synthesis note.** Very strong cross-domain failure evidence; controls remain context-specific.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Lower probability that optimisation improves the dashboard while degrading the system or shifting harm outside the model boundary.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** The optimisation surface improves while real consequence worsens; post-hoc model revision makes a preferred alternative appear optimal.

**Historical origin.** Goodhart/Campbell effects, principal-agent and performance-measurement criticism, algorithmic fairness and model-governance research.

**How the property evolved.** Metric criticism expanded into causal/performance monitoring, fairness/externality constraints, performative prediction and governed model revision.

**Important criticisms.** Anti-gaming controls themselves can create complexity and new gaming surfaces; the mature form prefers direct consequences and plural indicators where feasible.

**Known failure modes.** Threshold gaming, queue hiding, backlog reclassification, schedule padding, cost shifting, dropped constraints, selective scenarios, and post-hoc weights.

**Lineage class.** CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

**Evolved form in this source.** Targets are treated as interventions in a socio-technical system, with protected provenance and independent consequences that can invalidate the metric.

**Mechanism.** Maintain decision-frame and model-change provenance; use independent outcome measures, anti-gaming audits, frozen-before-result assumptions where warranted, distributional checks and explicit classification of legitimate revision versus evaluator capture.

**Model assumptions.** The proxy remains causally and institutionally linked to the valued outcome after it becomes a target.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use a direct outcome plus one counter-metric and inspect boundary cases/reclassifications; avoid composite scores where direct authority suffices.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Proxy, target, constraint, distributional impact, externalities, and opportunities for reclassification or cost shifting are explicit.

**Open questions.** How can model governance detect strategic omissions when the evaluator and decision owner share incentives?

**Original form.** Set operational targets and monitor performance against them.

**Primary sources.** S105; S106; S107; S108; S109; S110; S096; S097

**Problem it addressed.** Once control, reward or legitimacy depends on a metric/model, actors can reclassify work, shift costs, change weights, omit constraints or select scenarios to manufacture success.

**Property id.** P053

**Property name.** Detect and resist objective, constraint, data, queue, and reporting gaming

**Relationship class.** CONVERGENT_PROPERTY

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Independent outcome measurement, audit capacity, metric rotation or retirement, and exception review are funded.

**Trigger or context.** High-stakes KPIs, allocation rules, service levels, ranked alternatives, algorithmic control, incentives or politically contested optimisation.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Strategic adaptation and feedback-induced distribution shift are expected rather than treated as noise.

## P054 — Validate data-driven and decision-focused models in the deployed decision loop

**Ceremony vs property.** AI, differentiable solvers or end-to-end training are not evolved endpoints; demonstrated decision value under valid constraints is.

**Contrary evidence.** S096; S097; S105; S110

**Critical sources.** S096; S097; S105; S110

**Status at this source freeze.** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Training/deployment provenance, censoring, intervention feedback, leakage, shift, uncertainty, labels, and representativeness are assessed.

**Decision or consumer.** The downstream decision owner, operators, and affected stakeholders.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Endogenous/biased data, shift, selective labels, decision feedback, misspecified constraints or optimiser exploitation produce brittle or harmful recommendations out of sample.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** The downstream decision owner must approve the loss, constraints, training regime, deployment policy, monitoring, and fallback authority.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S069; S105

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** VERY_HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** MODERATE

**Field or operational strength.** LOW

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** MODERATE

**Implementation case strength.** MODERATE

**Replication or multi study strength.** LOW

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** HIGH

**Synthesis note.** Rapid formal development with uneven external, prospective and implementation evidence.

**Transferability strength.** MODERATE

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Prediction and learning effort targeted at consequential errors, with explicit monitoring and fallback when the deployment regime changes.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Endogenous/biased data, shift, selective labels, decision feedback, misspecified constraints or optimiser exploitation produce brittle or harmful recommendations out of sample.

**Historical origin.** Predict-then-optimise, decision-focused learning, performative prediction and recent AI–OR integration.

**How the property evolved.** Separated prediction/optimisation expanded into decision-focused training, differentiable optimisation, performative feedback and learned ambiguity sets, while deployment validation burdens increased.

**Important criticisms.** Decision-focused learning and RL can improve formal downstream loss under benchmark assumptions, but current external-validity, safety, scalability and implementation evidence remains uneven.

**Known failure modes.** Better prediction metrics but worse decisions, overfitting a decision layer, brittle constraints, hidden feedback, uncalibrated ambiguity sets, and benchmark-to-field transfer claims.

**Lineage class.** DATA_DRIVEN_OPTIMISATION_TRANSLATION

**Evolved form in this source.** A learned model is one component in a current, monitored decision system; the evidence claim is prospective consequence under the deployed loop, not benchmark loss alone.

**Mechanism.** Use held-out and prospective decision-consequence evaluation; test shift, feedback, calibration, constraint violations and counterfactual baselines; compare simple predict-then-optimise and heuristics; monitor after deployment with authority and rollback.

**Model assumptions.** Training data support the deployment regime; the optimisation layer is differentiable/solvable as claimed; constraints and ambiguity sets remain meaningful.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Prefer a transparent calibrated predictor plus robust/simple decision rule when end-to-end learning has no demonstrated incremental decision value.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Out-of-sample decision consequence, safety, fairness, robustness, compute and implementation cost—not prediction score alone—define value.

**Open questions.** Which prospective evaluation designs identify decision benefit when deployment changes the data-generating process?

**Original form.** Train predictors or end-to-end models to improve downstream optimisation loss rather than prediction accuracy alone.

**Primary sources.** S067; S068; S069; S096; S097; S120; S121; S103

**Problem it addressed.** Historical empirical risk or differentiable surrogate performance is treated as proof of deployed decision improvement.

**Property id.** P054

**Property name.** Validate data-driven and decision-focused models in the deployed decision loop

**Relationship class.** DOMAIN_TRANSLATION

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Training and solve cost, latency, monitoring, retraining, fallback, and human review are part of the engineering case.

**Trigger or context.** Learned predictions, costs, constraints, policies or surrogate solvers materially control operational decisions.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Distribution shift, endogenous data, model error, uncertainty-set calibration, nonstationarity and feedback are first-class.

## P055 — Monitor realised consequences and retire or revise models after drift

**Ceremony vs property.** A monitoring dashboard is optional; currentness evidence, outcome feedback and retirement authority are the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S096; S097; S105

**Critical sources.** S098; S101; S102; S105

**Status at this source freeze.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Live data freshness, local constraints, exceptions, incentives, override behaviour, and post-decision outcomes are observable.

**Decision or consumer.** The people or automated systems that act, plus the authority that owns exceptions and consequences.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Drift, changed incentives, altered service processes or new constraints silently invalidate the decision logic; failure is blamed on execution without testing the model.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** The operational authority, data owner, model owner, affected users, and override/escalation rights must be explicit.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S093; S100; S104; S124

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** HIGH

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** MODERATE

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** LOW

**Synthesis note.** Strong lifecycle property supported by implementation and feedback evidence.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Recommendations that survive contact with authority, workflow, incentives, data latency, and operator knowledge.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Drift, changed incentives, altered service processes or new constraints silently invalidate the decision logic; failure is blamed on execution without testing the model.

**Historical origin.** Operational OR implementation, rolling planning, forecast evaluation, model-risk governance and feedback-aware decision systems.

**How the property evolved.** One-time implementation studies expanded into model lifecycle governance, online evaluation, drift monitoring, performative-feedback analysis and explicit retirement.

**Important criticisms.** Adoption is not proof of validity and resistance is not proof of irrationality; implementation can reveal missing constraints or conflicting objectives.

**Known failure modes.** Technically superior but unusable recommendations, shadow systems, stale models, selective compliance, unowned overrides, and blaming users for revealed model defects.

**Lineage class.** CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

**Evolved form in this source.** Validity is time-bounded and consequence-tested; a model remains authoritative only while owned evidence supports its current decision use.

**Mechanism.** Define outcome and leading indicators, assumption/currentness checks, override and incident channels, review cadence/event triggers, version ownership, rollback and retirement criteria; distinguish model, data and implementation defects.

**Model assumptions.** Operators can execute the recommendation, incentives do not systematically defeat it, and the model remains current after deployment.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** For one-off reversible choices, record the outcome and a bounded reopen trigger; do not establish permanent monitoring machinery.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The implementable consequence, not mathematical recommendation alone, is the success criterion.

**Open questions.** How quickly can drift be distinguished from noise without causing excessive model or schedule churn?

**Original form.** Compare implemented recommendations with operational outcomes and update when assumptions no longer hold.

**Primary sources.** S063; S070; S093; S096; S097; S100; S101; S104; S124

**Problem it addressed.** A validated model, schedule, forecast, queue policy or metric becomes stale while its output continues to control action.

**Property id.** P055

**Property name.** Monitor realised consequences and retire or revise models after drift

**Relationship class.** CONVERGENT_PROPERTY

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Training, integration, change effort, exception handling, maintenance, and feedback capacity are included.

**Trigger or context.** Recurring or automated decisions, long-lived models, changing demand/capacity, adaptive adversaries, or high-consequence recommendations.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Human adaptation, workarounds, changing incentives, trust calibration, and organisational drift are treated as model uncertainty.

## P056 — Preserve near-optimal alternatives and portfolios when model discrimination is weak

**Ceremony vs property.** Enumerating every near-optimum is unnecessary; preserving materially indistinguishable choices and flexibility is the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S089

**Critical sources.** S070; S089

**Status at this source freeze.** OPTIMISATION_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Coefficients, feasible sets, capacities, costs, forecasts, and current state have provenance and uncertainty appropriate to their leverage on the solution.

**Decision or consumer.** The owner of the allocation, design, policy, or schedule and the operators who must implement it.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** The nominal winner is fragile, hard to implement or dominated after small parameter changes, while acceptable options and flexibility are discarded.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** The decision owner must approve the represented objective, constraints, alternatives, tolerances, and implementation rules; solver output is advice unless authority is explicitly automated.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S073

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** MODERATE

**Empirical comparative strength.** LOW

**Field or operational strength.** MODERATE

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** LOW

**Synthesis note.** Formally straightforward and operationally compelling; comparative implementation evidence is limited.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Transparent resource trade-offs and high-value improvements in complex feasible spaces where simpler discrimination is inadequate.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** The nominal winner is fragile, hard to implement or dominated after small parameter changes, while acceptable options and flexibility are discarded.

**Historical origin.** Multiple optima in mathematical programming, Pareto sets, sensitivity analysis, robust optimisation and analysis-of-alternatives practice.

**How the property evolved.** Single-solution optimisation broadened toward Pareto fronts, sensitivity regions, robust/near-optimal sets, scenario portfolios and human selection among transparent options.

**Important criticisms.** Formal optimality is conditional on the model; small optimality gaps can coexist with large real-world regret, and more exact algorithms do not repair misspecification.

**Known failure modes.** Optimising the wrong objective, infeasible or unenforceable constraints, numerical false precision, brittle point estimates, local optimum/global harm, and solver optimality worship.

**Lineage class.** HYBRID

**Evolved form in this source.** Optimisation narrows and structures the choice set; it does not manufacture certainty beyond model resolution.

**Mechanism.** Generate epsilon/near-optimal sets, efficient portfolios or materially indistinguishable alternatives; compare implementation, robustness, option value and stakeholder consequences; retain choice until evidence discriminates.

**Model assumptions.** Functional form, linearity/convexity, integrality, independence, horizon, decomposition, and numerical tolerances are stated and tested where material.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Report ties and break-even ranges rather than force a single rank; keep two feasible options alive when cheap.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The mathematical objective is traceable to valued consequences; hard constraints are enforceable; penalties, scaling, and proxies are challengeable.

**Open questions.** How should an epsilon for ‘near-optimal’ be tied to consequence and model uncertainty rather than arbitrary percentage?

**Original form.** Report the optimiser's selected optimum, alternative optima, or an efficient frontier.

**Primary sources.** S007; S025; S041; S073; S077; S082

**Problem it addressed.** A tiny modelled objective difference is converted into a categorical organisational commitment despite uncertainty and unmodelled implementation attributes.

**Property id.** P056

**Property name.** Preserve near-optimal alternatives and portfolios when model discrimination is weak

**Relationship class.** HYBRID_RESOLUTION

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Formulation, solve time, compute, data maintenance, implementation effort, and switching/reconfiguration costs are part of the decision.

**Trigger or context.** Close objective values, substantial parameter/weight uncertainty, several feasible implementations, or high switching/irreversibility cost.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Sensitivity, out-of-sample performance, scenario or ambiguity analysis, and model-form uncertainty are addressed according to consequence.

## P057 — A named solver or optimisation algorithm is not a universal engineering property

**Ceremony vs property.** Solver branding, certificates and dashboards are artefacts; they are not independent evidence of decision quality.

**Contrary evidence.** S070; S089; S098

**Critical sources.** S070; S089; S098

**Status at this source freeze.** CEREMONY_NOT_GENERAL_PROPERTY

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Evidence must address whether the artefact changes decisions or operations, not merely whether it exists or is detailed.

**Decision or consumer.** Only a live decision or operational consumer can justify the practice.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** A wrong objective, missing alternative, invalid distribution or infeasible implementation is solved faster or more exactly without improving the real decision.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** No artefact or named method acquires authority merely by being produced; an accountable decision owner remains required.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S062

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** MODERATE

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** LOW

**Synthesis note.** Strong ceremony-stripping disposition: algorithms remain necessary in context but not universal properties.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Lower bureaucracy and retention of the transferable mechanism without mandatory branding or artefact form.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** A wrong objective, missing alternative, invalid distribution or infeasible implementation is solved faster or more exactly without improving the real decision.

**Historical origin.** The proliferation of simplex, branch-and-bound, decomposition, convex, dynamic, heuristic and metaheuristic solver families.

**How the property evolved.** Algorithm families became more powerful and specialised, but mature practice increasingly separates computational optimality from model and implementation validity.

**Important criticisms.** The named practice may still be useful in a bounded context; rejection applies to its elevation into a universal property.

**Known failure modes.** Tool worship, compliance-only production, false precision, and replacing model validity with algorithm or artefact completion.

**Lineage class.** MATHEMATICAL_PROGRAMMING_LINEAGE

**Evolved form in this source.** No solver family is admitted as a general property; the surviving property is fit-for-decision computation under verified formulation and bounded cost.

**Mechanism.** Treat algorithms as conditional implementations chosen after framing, formulation, assumption and value-of-computation tests; compare simple rules and preserve solver-independent validation.

**Model assumptions.** Method-specific assumptions are not silently universalised.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Enumeration, dominance, a direct constraint, rule, greedy heuristic or manual calculation when it settles the decision reliably.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The underlying decision consequence must be recovered from the ritualised proxy or artefact.

**Open questions.** Which solver-level diagnostics most effectively expose formulation defects rather than merely numerical difficulty?

**Original form.** Select an algorithm suited to a represented mathematical structure and computational budget.

**Primary sources.** S007; S010; S014; S017; S020; S030; S037; S071; S072

**Problem it addressed.** Solver use or algorithmic sophistication is treated as evidence that the underlying decision has been engineered.

**Trigger or context.** Only when a formal model has a live decision consumer and computational complexity materially prevents useful discrimination.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Uncertainty hidden by deterministic artefacts, precise scores, or repeated computation is surfaced.

## P058 — A fixed deterministic PERT/CPM/Gantt network is superseded as general schedule authority

**Ceremony vs property.** A Gantt/PERT/CPM artefact is optional; current dependency/resource truth is the property.

## P064 — A giant integrated model is useful only when integration value exceeds opacity and maintenance cost

**Ceremony vs property.** Model size and data volume are not completeness; maintained decision-relevant coupling is the property.

**Contrary evidence.** S098; S099; S101

**Critical sources.** S098; S099; S101; S102

**Status at this source freeze.** USEFUL_BUT_EASILY_BUREAUCRATISED

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Live data freshness, local constraints, exceptions, incentives, override behaviour, and post-decision outcomes are observable.

**Decision or consumer.** The people or automated systems that act, plus the authority that owns exceptions and consequences.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** The model becomes stale before use, hides domain-specific uncertainty, centralises illegitimate authority, or produces output no operator can implement or diagnose.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** The operational authority, data owner, model owner, affected users, and override/escalation rights must be explicit.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S100; S101; S112

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** MODERATE

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** HIGH

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** LOW

**Synthesis note.** Integration can prevent local failure but is strongly bureaucracy- and maintenance-sensitive.

**Transferability strength.** HIGH

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Recommendations that survive contact with authority, workflow, incentives, data latency, and operator knowledge.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** The model becomes stale before use, hides domain-specific uncertainty, centralises illegitimate authority, or produces output no operator can implement or diagnose.

**Historical origin.** Large-scale mathematical programming, military systems analysis, enterprise planning and integrated simulation/optimisation.

**How the property evolved.** Monolithic planning models were supplemented by decomposition, hierarchical/bi-level optimisation, modular simulation and problem-structuring approaches.

**Important criticisms.** Adoption is not proof of validity and resistance is not proof of irrationality; implementation can reveal missing constraints or conflicting objectives.

**Known failure modes.** Technically superior but unusable recommendations, shadow systems, stale models, selective compliance, unowned overrides, and blaming users for revealed model defects.

**Lineage class.** HYBRID

**Evolved form in this source.** Integration scope is itself a value-of-information/maintenance decision; modularity is preferred unless coupling evidence warrants central representation.

**Mechanism.** Integrate only decision-coupled variables; use modular interfaces, decomposition, local validation, provenance and ownership; test whether a smaller model or coordination prices/rules provides the same discrimination.

**Model assumptions.** Operators can execute the recommendation, incentives do not systematically defeat it, and the model remains current after deployment.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Coordinate a few binding interfaces, budgets or bottlenecks between smaller models rather than build an all-encompassing digital representation.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The implementable consequence, not mathematical recommendation alone, is the success criterion.

**Open questions.** How can the marginal value of integration be measured before incurring irreversible model-maintenance cost?

**Original form.** Represent many coupled decisions and constraints in one model to avoid inconsistent local optimisation.

**Primary sources.** S005; S006; S020; S030; S098; S099; S101; S112

**Problem it addressed.** Integration is pursued as completeness, creating an opaque, brittle model with no owner, slow updates and unchallengeable assumptions.

**Property id.** P064

**Property name.** A giant integrated model is useful only when integration value exceeds opacity and maintenance cost

**Relationship class.** SYSTEMS_ANALYSIS_IMPORT_OR_SHARED_ANCESTRY

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Training, integration, change effort, exception handling, maintenance, and feedback capacity are included.

**Trigger or context.** Strong cross-system coupling where local decisions materially externalise cost and a shared model can be maintained and governed.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Human adaptation, workarounds, changing incentives, trust calibration, and organisational drift are treated as model uncertainty.

**Requalification.** **Claim ids.** C064

## P065 — Universal transfer of parametric queue, inventory, and scheduling formulas to knowledge or agent work remains unresolved

**Ceremony vs property.** Renaming tasks ‘jobs’ and workers ‘servers’ does not establish a queueing model.

**Contrary evidence.** S052; S113

**Critical sources.** S052; S113

**Status at this source freeze.** UNRESOLVED

**Data or measurement preconditions.** Evidence must address whether the artefact changes decisions or operations, not merely whether it exists or is detailed.

**Decision or consumer.** Only a live decision or operational consumer can justify the practice.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** A neat M/M/1, EOQ or machine-scheduling analogy ignores strategic task selection, learning, retries, shared acceptance, endogenous service, quality variation or changing task definitions.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** No artefact or named method acquires authority merely by being produced; an accountable decision owner remains required.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S053; S057

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** VERY_HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** HIGH

**Empirical comparative strength.** LOW

**Field or operational strength.** LOW

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** LOW

**Replication or multi study strength.** LOW

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** MODERATE

**Synthesis note.** Relevant literature was searched; qualitative transfer is supported, universal quantitative transfer is not.

**Transferability strength.** LOW

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** Lower bureaucracy and retention of the transferable mechanism without mandatory branding or artefact form.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** A neat M/M/1, EOQ or machine-scheduling analogy ignores strategic task selection, learning, retries, shared acceptance, endogenous service, quality variation or changing task definitions.

**Historical origin.** Classical queueing, inventory and scheduling models and their later metaphorical application to software, knowledge work and AI-agent systems.

**How the property evolved.** Classical formulas expanded into networks, time-varying/heavy-tailed queues, learning-augmented algorithms and domain-specific empirical models; general transfer evidence remains incomplete.

**Important criticisms.** The named practice may still be useful in a bounded context; rejection applies to its elevation into a universal property.

**Known failure modes.** Tool worship, compliance-only production, false precision, and replacing model validity with algorithm or artefact completion.

**Lineage class.** ONLY_ANALOGOUS

**Evolved form in this source.** Congestion and constraint reasoning are retained; exact formula transfer remains conditional and must be validated domain by domain.

**Mechanism.** Separate qualitative invariants—finite capacity, variability, WIP, bottlenecks, dependency, recourse—from parametric claims; empirically test arrivals, service, routing, feedback and consequence before quantitative transfer.

**Model assumptions.** Method-specific assumptions are not silently universalised.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use direct WIP/backlog, elapsed-time, bottleneck and conflict observations; avoid distributional formulas until diagnostics support them.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** The underlying decision consequence must be recovered from the ritualised proxy or artefact.

**Open questions.** Which empirical signatures justify particular queueing or scheduling abstractions for heterogeneous human–AI work?; How should endogenous task generation and shared validation resources be represented?

**Original form.** Map arrivals, service times, servers, buffers, precedence and costs to a new operational domain and apply established formulas or algorithms.

**Primary sources.** S002; S003; S018; S019; S050; S051; S052; S055; S057; S113; S119

**Problem it addressed.** Transferable congestion/dependency logic is conflated with validity of specific stationarity, independence, homogeneity, service-discipline and cost assumptions.

**Property id.** P065

**Property name.** Universal transfer of parametric queue, inventory, and scheduling formulas to knowledge or agent work remains unresolved

**Relationship class.** UNRESOLVED_TENSION

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Ceremony cost and opportunity cost are counted.

**Trigger or context.** Novel service, software, knowledge or agentic systems where queue/capacity/schedule effects are plausible but the stochastic process is not established.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Uncertainty hidden by deterministic artefacts, precise scores, or repeated computation is surfaced.

**Requalification.** **Claim ids.** C065-U; C065-F

## P066 — Use maintenance and replacement policies only with a credible failure-state and renewal model

**Ceremony vs property.** Predictive-maintenance branding, sensor volume or a fixed preventive calendar is not the property; consequence-grounded intervention timing is.

**Contrary evidence.** S089; S132

**Critical sources.** S089; S132

**Status at this source freeze.** DOMAIN_SPECIFIC

**Data or measurement preconditions.** State observations, transition/feedback data, delay, censoring, intervention effects, and nonstationarity are measured or bounded.

**Decision or consumer.** The operator or automated controller with explicit authority and guardrails.

**Decision or operational failure mode.** Premature replacement wastes capacity and parts; delayed intervention causes avoidable failure; sensor/prognostic error or common-cause dependence invalidates a nominal threshold policy.

**Decision owner or authority preconditions.** The policy owner must control observations, update cadence, action authority, exploration limits, and switching or lock-in costs.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** A live decision, a consequence-bearing consumer, and enough boundary information to distinguish alternatives.

**Empirical or domain evidence.** S132

**Evidence profile at this source freeze.** **Assumption sensitivity.** HIGH

**Contrary evidence strength.** MODERATE

**Empirical comparative strength.** MODERATE

**Field or operational strength.** HIGH

**Formal or theoretical strength.** HIGH

**Historical provenance strength.** HIGH

**Implementation case strength.** MODERATE

**Replication or multi study strength.** MODERATE

**Simulation or model evidence strength.** HIGH

**Synthesis note.** Distinct and mature within reliability-intensive domains, but not a universal operations property.

**Transferability strength.** LOW

**Expected payoff asserted at this source scope.** State-contingent action, controlled learning, and timely re-optimisation without pretending that a static plan predicts the future.

**Failure mode it tries to prevent.** Premature replacement wastes capacity and parts; delayed intervention causes avoidable failure; sensor/prognostic error or common-cause dependence invalidates a nominal threshold policy.

**Historical origin.** Renewal theory, age/block replacement, reliability economics and modern condition-based maintenance optimisation.

**How the property evolved.** Age and block replacement models expanded into imperfect repair, multi-component and condition-based policies, joint operations-maintenance scheduling, prognostics and partially observed sequential control.

**Important criticisms.** Formal regret or policy guarantees are environment- and model-dependent; reinforcement learning is one translation, not the universal endpoint.

**Known failure modes.** One-shot plans in changing systems, oscillatory replanning, reward misspecification, unsafe exploration, delayed-feedback bias, and lock-in ignored by rolling optimisation.

**Lineage class.** DOMAIN_SPECIFIC

**Evolved form in this source.** Maintenance is a state-contingent reliability-and-capacity decision whose policy is validated against actual failure behaviour, observation quality, downtime and resource coupling.

**Mechanism.** Define failure states and consequence, deterioration/renewal assumptions, imperfect repair, inspection and observation quality, maintenance resource windows, spare capacity and recourse; compare corrective, preventive and condition-based policies out of sample.

**Model assumptions.** State sufficiency, transition structure, feedback timing, horizon/discounting, exploration safety, and policy class are explicit.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Use manufacturer/field failure evidence, a conservative inspection interval, direct condition threshold or run-to-failure where consequence is low and replacement is cheap; avoid predictive optimisation without incremental value.

**Objective and constraint preconditions.** Immediate reward/cost, future state, learning value, safety/constraint violation, switching cost, and long-horizon consequences are represented.

**Open questions.** How transferable are learned condition thresholds across operating regimes and maintenance organisations?; When does predictive maintenance outperform simple age/inspection rules after sensing and model-lifecycle cost?

**Original form.** Choose preventive replacement or repair timing by balancing failure, downtime, inspection, maintenance and replacement costs under a life/failure distribution.

**Primary sources.** S131; S132

**Problem it addressed.** Calendar maintenance, run-to-failure, or predictive-maintenance output is treated as universally efficient without establishing deterioration, failure consequence, observability or repair effectiveness.

**Property id.** P066

**Property name.** Use maintenance and replacement policies only with a credible failure-state and renewal model

**Relationship class.** INDUSTRIAL_ENGINEERING_HYBRID

**Resource or capacity preconditions.** Sensing, computation, experimentation capacity, reconfiguration effort, and operational slack constrain adaptation.

**Trigger or context.** Assets or services with material failure consequence, repeated life cycles, observable condition or age dependence, and controllable maintenance/replacement action.

**Uncertainty preconditions.** Unknown dynamics, delayed feedback, drift, partial observability, and exploration risk are treated as first-class burdens.

**Requalification.** **Claim ids.** C066
